### Large Language Model Hacking: Quantifying the Hidden Risks of Using LLMs for Text Annotation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08825v1)
- **Authors**: Joachim Baumann, Paul Röttger, Aleksandra Urman, Albert Wendsjö, Flor Miriam Plaza-del-Arco, Johannes B. Gruber, Dirk Hovy
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) are rapidly transforming social science research by enabling the automation of labor-intensive tasks like data annotation and text analysis. However, LLM outputs vary significantly depending on the implementation choices made by researchers (e.g., model selection, prompting strategy, or temperature settings). Such variation can introduce systematic biases and random errors, which propagate to downstream analyses and cause Type I, Type II, Type S, or Type M errors. We call this LLM hacking. We quantify the risk of LLM hacking by replicating 37 data annotation tasks from 21 published social science research studies with 18 different models. Analyzing 13 million LLM labels, we test 2,361 realistic hypotheses to measure how plausible researcher choices affect statistical conclusions. We find incorrect conclusions based on LLM-annotated data in approximately one in three hypotheses for state-of-the-art models, and in half the hypotheses for small language models. While our findings show that higher task performance and better general model capabilities reduce LLM hacking risk, even highly accurate models do not completely eliminate it. The risk of LLM hacking decreases as effect sizes increase, indicating the need for more rigorous verification of findings near significance thresholds. Our extensive analysis of LLM hacking mitigation techniques emphasizes the importance of human annotations in reducing false positive findings and improving model selection. Surprisingly, common regression estimator correction techniques are largely ineffective in reducing LLM hacking risk, as they heavily trade off Type I vs. Type II errors. Beyond accidental errors, we find that intentional LLM hacking is unacceptably simple. With few LLMs and just a handful of prompt paraphrases, anything can be presented as statistically significant.
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

This paper investigates "LLM hacking," the risk of generating incorrect conclusions in social science research due to variations in LLM implementation choices (model selection, prompting, etc.) when automating tasks like data annotation. The authors replicated 37 data annotation tasks from published studies using 18 LLMs, analyzing 13 million labels. They found that approximately one in three hypotheses had incorrect conclusions based on state-of-the-art LLMs, rising to half for smaller models. The risk decreases with better model performance and larger effect sizes, but even highly accurate models don't eliminate it. Human annotation helps reduce false positives and improve model selection, but common regression corrections are ineffective. Alarmingly, the paper also demonstrates how easily statistical significance can be manipulated through intentional LLM hacking.

**Critical Evaluation:**

This paper tackles a crucial and timely issue: the reliability and validity of social science research that leverages LLMs for tasks like data annotation. The core contribution lies in its systematic quantification of the risks associated with LLM implementation choices, termed "LLM hacking."

**Novelty:**

The paper presents a novel framework for understanding and quantifying the problem. While others have noted the variability of LLM outputs, this work goes further by:

*   **Defining and formalizing the concept of "LLM hacking"** in a research context.
*   **Empirically demonstrating the frequency and magnitude of the problem** across a wide range of real-world social science tasks.
*   **Quantifying the impact of different LLM choices** (model selection, prompting, temperature) on statistical conclusions, including Type I and Type II error rates.
*   **Investigating the effectiveness of mitigation techniques** like human annotation and regression corrections.
*   **Highlighting the potential for intentional manipulation** of LLM outputs to achieve desired statistical results.

These elements, taken together, constitute a novel and substantial contribution. Existing research often focuses on the performance of LLMs in isolation or on specific tasks. This paper, however, takes a broader, more critical perspective by examining the potential for LLM variability to undermine the validity of scientific inferences.

**Significance:**

The paper's findings have significant implications for social science research, especially given the increasing reliance on LLMs. The key takeaways are:

*   **LLM-generated data cannot be treated as a "black box."** Researchers must be aware of the potential for implementation choices to influence results and introduce biases.
*   **Rigorous validation and sensitivity analyses are crucial.** This includes exploring different model configurations, prompting strategies, and temperature settings, as well as comparing LLM-generated data to human annotations.
*   **Common statistical correction methods may not be sufficient** to address the problem of LLM hacking, as they may simply trade off Type I and Type II errors.
*   **The ease with which LLM outputs can be manipulated raises ethical concerns** about the potential for researchers to intentionally bias results.

The paper's findings challenge the uncritical acceptance of LLM-generated data and call for a more rigorous and transparent approach to research. It prompts a necessary conversation about methodological best practices for using LLMs in social science.

**Strengths:**

*   **Large-scale empirical analysis:** The study is based on a substantial dataset of 13 million LLM labels, providing strong statistical power.
*   **Replication of real-world tasks:** The use of data annotation tasks from published studies enhances the ecological validity of the findings.
*   **Comprehensive analysis of different LLM choices:** The paper systematically explores the impact of model selection, prompting strategies, and temperature settings.
*   **Investigation of mitigation techniques:** The evaluation of human annotation and regression corrections provides practical guidance for researchers.
*   **Clear and accessible writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, even for readers who are not experts in LLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited scope of tasks:** While the study includes a variety of data annotation tasks, it may not be representative of all social science applications of LLMs. Some more qualitative analyses of text generation might not be fully captured.
*   **Focus on annotation:** While annotation is a core task, other crucial uses of LLMs such as literature review or hypothesis generation are not directly considered and might present different challenges.
*   **Potential for task-specific effects:** The findings may be specific to the particular tasks and datasets used in the study.
*   **Limited exploration of advanced mitigation techniques:** The paper focuses on human annotation and regression corrections. Further research is needed to explore other potential mitigation techniques, such as ensemble methods or adversarial training.
*   **The choice of models analyzed:** While the paper used a variety of LLMs, the rapid development of the field means that newer models might have different characteristics. Future work should consider the impact of the latest generation of LLMs.

**Conclusion:**

Overall, this paper represents a significant and timely contribution to the field of social science research. Its systematic analysis of "LLM hacking" highlights the risks associated with using LLMs for data annotation and provides valuable guidance for researchers seeking to mitigate these risks. Despite some limitations in scope, the paper's findings are robust and have important implications for methodological best practices. The demonstration of how easily the system can be "hacked" is particularly impactful. It should be considered essential reading for anyone considering using LLMs in their research.

### RewardDance: Reward Scaling in Visual Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08826v1)
- **Authors**: Jie Wu, Yu Gao, Zilyu Ye, Ming Li, Liang Li, Hanzhong Guo, Jie Liu, Zeyue Xue, Xiaoxia Hou, Wei Liu, Yan Zeng, Weilin Huang
- **Abstract**: Reward Models (RMs) are critical for improving generation models via Reinforcement Learning (RL), yet the RM scaling paradigm in visual generation remains largely unexplored. It primarily due to fundamental limitations in existing approaches: CLIP-based RMs suffer from architectural and input modality constraints, while prevalent Bradley-Terry losses are fundamentally misaligned with the next-token prediction mechanism of Vision-Language Models (VLMs), hindering effective scaling. More critically, the RLHF optimization process is plagued by Reward Hacking issue, where models exploit flaws in the reward signal without improving true quality. To address these challenges, we introduce RewardDance, a scalable reward modeling framework that overcomes these barriers through a novel generative reward paradigm. By reformulating the reward score as the model's probability of predicting a "yes" token, indicating that the generated image outperforms a reference image according to specific criteria, RewardDance intrinsically aligns reward objectives with VLM architectures. This alignment unlocks scaling across two dimensions: (1) Model Scaling: Systematic scaling of RMs up to 26 billion parameters; (2) Context Scaling: Integration of task-specific instructions, reference examples, and chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RewardDance significantly surpasses state-of-the-art methods in text-to-image, text-to-video, and image-to-video generation. Crucially, we resolve the persistent challenge of "reward hacking": Our large-scale RMs exhibit and maintain high reward variance during RL fine-tuning, proving their resistance to hacking and ability to produce diverse, high-quality outputs. It greatly relieves the mode collapse problem that plagues smaller models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "RewardDance: Reward Scaling in Visual Generation" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RewardDance, a framework for scaling reward models (RMs) in visual generation.  It addresses limitations of existing RMs (CLIP-based or using Bradley-Terry loss) by proposing a generative reward paradigm where the reward is framed as the probability of predicting a "yes" token indicating preference of one image over another. This paradigm is better aligned with the architecture of vision-language models (VLMs). The framework enables scaling along two dimensions: model size (1B to 26B parameters) and context (task-specific instructions, reference examples, chain-of-thought reasoning). Experiments show that RewardDance outperforms existing methods in text-to-image, text-to-video, and image-to-video generation, and is more resistant to reward hacking. The authors demonstrate a strong correlation between RM size and generation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to reward modeling in visual generation by framing it as a generative task. Aligning the RM objective with the native architecture of VLMs is a good idea and seems to produce promising results. Scaling RMs in both model size and context is also a significant contribution, as most prior work explored only one or the other. The findings about reward hacking and variance during RL fine-tuning are valuable. The paper claims to resolve reward hacking however, it demonstrates a robust resistance but not complete resolution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial bottleneck in improving diffusion models and other generative models by enabling more effective use of reinforcement learning.  Scaling RMs is a critical step for achieving higher-quality and more diverse visual outputs. The results demonstrate a clear improvement over existing state-of-the-art methods. The ability to incorporate context (instructions, examples, reasoning) opens new avenues for RM design. Furthermore, the empirical observations of performance saturation with high accuracy ID accuracy RMs but higher generalization by OOD is a significant contribution to our understanding of RMs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong Empirical Results:**  The paper presents extensive experiments across multiple tasks and models, demonstrating the effectiveness of RewardDance.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing reward modeling techniques.
*   **Well-Motivated Approach:**  The proposed generative paradigm and scaling strategy are well-motivated.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The ablation studies and comparisons to state-of-the-art models are thorough.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The computational cost of training and using large-scale RMs, especially with increased context, is high. This might limit the adoption of RewardDance in resource-constrained environments.
*   **Data Dependency:** Like most RM approaches, RewardDance relies on large amounts of human preference data. The quality and bias of this data can significantly impact the performance of the RM.
*   **Generality:** While the paper demonstrates strong results on a variety of tasks, it would be beneficial to explore the generality of RewardDance on a wider range of visual generation problems.
*   **Limited Reward Hack Resolution.** As mentioned before, the paper demonstrates resistance but claims complete resolution of reward hacking issue.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of visual generation by introducing a scalable and effective reward modeling framework. The idea of aligning RM objectives with VLM architectures through a generative paradigm is novel and well-supported by experimental results. The comprehensive evaluation and ablation studies further strengthen the findings. However, the high computational cost and dependency on human preference data are limitations. Furthermore, while the approach exhibits a resistance to reward hacking, its complete resolution is yet to be completely proven. The novelty, significance and clear advancement in the field of visual generation justify a score of 8.

**Score: 8**

- **Score**: 8/10

### A Survey of Reinforcement Learning for Large Reasoning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08827v1)
- **Authors**: Kaiyan Zhang, Yuxin Zuo, Bingxiang He, Youbang Sun, Runze Liu, Che Jiang, Yuchen Fan, Kai Tian, Guoli Jia, Pengfei Li, Yu Fu, Xingtai Lv, Yuchen Zhang, Sihang Zeng, Shang Qu, Haozhan Li, Shijie Wang, Yuru Wang, Xinwei Long, Fangfu Liu, Xiang Xu, Jiaze Ma, Xuekai Zhu, Ermo Hua, Yihao Liu, Zonglin Li, Huayu Chen, Xiaoye Qu, Yafu Li, Weize Chen, Zhenzhao Yuan, Junqi Gao, Dong Li, Zhiyuan Ma, Ganqu Cui, Zhiyuan Liu, Biqing Qi, Ning Ding, Bowen Zhou
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we survey recent advances in Reinforcement Learning (RL) for reasoning with Large Language Models (LLMs). RL has achieved remarkable success in advancing the frontier of LLM capabilities, particularly in addressing complex logical tasks such as mathematics and coding. As a result, RL has emerged as a foundational methodology for transforming LLMs into LRMs. With the rapid progress of the field, further scaling of RL for LRMs now faces foundational challenges not only in computational resources but also in algorithm design, training data, and infrastructure. To this end, it is timely to revisit the development of this domain, reassess its trajectory, and explore strategies to enhance the scalability of RL toward Artificial SuperIntelligence (ASI). In particular, we examine research applying RL to LLMs and LRMs for reasoning abilities, especially since the release of DeepSeek-R1, including foundational components, core problems, training resources, and downstream applications, to identify future opportunities and directions for this rapidly evolving area. We hope this review will promote future research on RL for broader reasoning models. Github: https://github.com/TsinghuaC3I/Awesome-RL-for-LRMs
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the survey paper:

**Summary:**

This survey paper explores recent advancements in reinforcement learning (RL) for reasoning with large language models (LLMs), termed Large Reasoning Models (LRMs). It argues that RL has become a key methodology for developing LRM capabilities, particularly in complex reasoning tasks like mathematics and coding. The survey delves into the foundational components of RL for LRMs, including reward design, policy optimization, and sampling strategies. It identifies key challenges such as computational resource constraints, algorithm design, training data, and infrastructure limitations. The paper also reviews training resources, downstream applications, and future directions in the field, emphasizing the need for scalable RL approaches towards achieving Artificial SuperIntelligence (ASI). The authors highlight significant milestones such as OpenAI's 01 and DeepSeek's R1 models, which demonstrate the power of RL with verifiable rewards.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main strength lies in its comprehensive and up-to-date coverage of a rapidly evolving field. However, the degree of novelty in the content itself is moderate. Similar surveys on RL and LLMs exist, but this paper distinguishes itself by specifically focusing on RL for reasoning and explicitly identifying the shift towards using RL not just for alignment, but to incentivize reasoning itself. The identification and taxonomy of components, problems, and resources are useful but not groundbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its timely intervention in a critical area of AI research. By consolidating information on RL-based LRM development, it offers valuable insights to researchers and practitioners. The identification of key challenges and future directions can help focus research efforts and promote innovation. The paper's discussion of scaling issues and the path toward ASI adds to its impact by framing the current state within a broader, long-term vision. Its release shortly after DeepSeek-R1 demonstrates a timely focus on emergent trends.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Coverage:** Provides a broad overview of relevant literature.
    *   **Timeliness:** Captures recent developments, including key models and techniques.
    *   **Clear Organization:** Presents information in a structured and accessible manner with well-defined sections.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** Identifies key challenges and suggests potential research directions.
    *   **Emphasis on Reasoning:** Differentiates itself from surveys focusing solely on alignment.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** The survey mainly describes existing approaches rather than providing in-depth theoretical analyses.
    *   **Lack of Critical Synthesis:** While comprehensive, the paper could benefit from a stronger critical synthesis of different approaches, highlighting their pros and cons in more detail.
    *   **Overly Optimistic View of ASI:** The framing of RL for LRMs as a direct path to ASI could be seen as somewhat speculative and oversimplified.
    *   **Limited Critical Assessment:** While comprehensive, the paper could be more critical in assessing individual papers, particularly in an emergent field where not all papers are equal in quality.

*   **Potential Influence:** This survey can influence future research by providing a consolidated overview of RL for LRMs, highlighting open problems, and suggesting promising research directions. It may also help standardize terminology and encourage the development of more robust evaluation metrics.

*   **Score:** 7

**Rationale:** While the paper provides a valuable and up-to-date resource for researchers in RL and LLMs, its novelty is incremental. It builds upon existing surveys and knowledge, but does so in a well-structured, comprehensive, and highly relevant way for a particular niche and moment in time. It is not a groundbreaking, transformative paper, but a timely and helpful contribution that is significantly helpful. The identification of fundamental challenges and potential avenues toward ASI adds to its significance.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Recurrence Meets Transformers for Universal Multimodal Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08897v1)
- **Authors**: Davide Caffagni, Sara Sarto, Marcella Cornia, Lorenzo Baraldi, Rita Cucchiara
- **Abstract**: With the rapid advancement of multimodal retrieval and its application in LLMs and multimodal LLMs, increasingly complex retrieval tasks have emerged. Existing methods predominantly rely on task-specific fine-tuning of vision-language models and are limited to single-modality queries or documents. In this paper, we propose ReT-2, a unified retrieval model that supports multimodal queries, composed of both images and text, and searches across multimodal document collections where text and images coexist. ReT-2 leverages multi-layer representations and a recurrent Transformer architecture with LSTM-inspired gating mechanisms to dynamically integrate information across layers and modalities, capturing fine-grained visual and textual details. We evaluate ReT-2 on the challenging M2KR and M-BEIR benchmarks across different retrieval configurations. Results demonstrate that ReT-2 consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance across diverse settings, while offering faster inference and reduced memory usage compared to prior approaches. When integrated into retrieval-augmented generation pipelines, ReT-2 also improves downstream performance on Encyclopedic-VQA and InfoSeek datasets. Our source code and trained models are publicly available at: https://github.com/aimagelab/ReT-2
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ReT-2, a novel recurrence-enhanced Transformer model designed for universal multimodal retrieval.  ReT-2 aims to address limitations in existing methods that are often task-specific, handle only single-modality queries/documents, and don't fully exploit multi-layer features. ReT-2 uses a recurrent Transformer architecture with LSTM-inspired gating mechanisms to dynamically integrate information from different layers and modalities (text and images). This enables it to capture fine-grained details often missed in deeper layers and to handle diverse retrieval tasks with multimodal queries and documents, including scenarios with missing modalities. The model's performance is evaluated on the M2KR and M-BEIR benchmarks, demonstrating state-of-the-art results, faster inference, and reduced memory usage compared to previous approaches. Furthermore, the paper shows how ReT-2 improves downstream performance in retrieval-augmented generation tasks on Encyclopedic-VQA and InfoSeek datasets. The source code and trained models are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper builds upon the authors' previous work, ReT, by introducing several architectural modifications focused on improving both efficiency and robustness. The key novel aspects include:

    *   A shared query-document encoder, replacing the separate encoders of ReT.
    *   Token reduction to a single token per modality, mitigating rank collapse.
    *   A simplified contrastive objective, eliminating the need for a fine-grained loss.
    *   Layer pruning, using only a subset of carefully selected layers to reduce computation.
    *   Global feature injection, adding a global contextual representation to the fused features.
    *   Extensive experimentation on several multimodal datasets

    While these changes are incremental, their combination leads to a significantly improved and more efficient model compared to its predecessor.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses an important and timely problem: the need for unified and flexible multimodal retrieval models that can handle diverse queries and document types. The growing importance of multimodal data, especially in the context of large language models, makes this a relevant area of research. The demonstrated improvements in retrieval accuracy, inference speed, and memory usage contribute to the practical usability of the model. The results on retrieval-augmented generation show that ReT-2 can also improve performance on downstream tasks.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides extensive experimental results on challenging and diverse benchmarks (M2KR and M-BEIR) across different retrieval configurations.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** Thorough ablation studies clearly demonstrate the impact of each architectural modification, providing valuable insights into the model's behavior.
    *   **Practical Efficiency:** The paper emphasizes not only accuracy but also computational efficiency, a crucial consideration for real-world deployment.
    *   **Improved Downstream Performance**: The method's usefulness extends beyond itself, improving the preformance of downstream tasks such as VQA.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvements:** While the architectural changes are effective, they are relatively incremental improvements over ReT rather than a radical departure.
    *   **MLLM Comparison:** It's important to note that ReT-2 still falls behind MLLM based retrievers such as LamRA.
    *   **Knowledge-Intensive Tasks:** ReT-2 only manages to outperform PreFLMR in InfoSeek. This suggests that the MLLM's training stage is still critical for the approach.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research in multimodal retrieval. The proposed architecture and the analysis of multi-layer feature integration and gating mechanisms provide a valuable framework for developing more efficient and robust retrieval models. The public availability of the code and models will facilitate further research and adoption.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of multimodal retrieval by introducing a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated model that improves upon existing approaches. The focus on both accuracy and efficiency is commendable. While the architectural changes are incremental, their combined impact is significant, and the experimental results are compelling. The analysis and insights provided in the paper contribute to a better understanding of multimodal feature integration. I'm giving a score of 7 because it is an incremental but solid improvement.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Diffusion-Based Action Recognition Generalizes to Untrained Domains
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08908v1)
- **Authors**: Rogerio Guimaraes, Frank Xiao, Pietro Perona, Markus Marks
- **Abstract**: Humans can recognize the same actions despite large context and viewpoint variations, such as differences between species (walking in spiders vs. horses), viewpoints (egocentric vs. third-person), and contexts (real life vs movies). Current deep learning models struggle with such generalization. We propose using features generated by a Vision Diffusion Model (VDM), aggregated via a transformer, to achieve human-like action recognition across these challenging conditions. We find that generalization is enhanced by the use of a model conditioned on earlier timesteps of the diffusion process to highlight semantic information over pixel level details in the extracted features. We experimentally explore the generalization properties of our approach in classifying actions across animal species, across different viewing angles, and different recording contexts. Our model sets a new state-of-the-art across all three generalization benchmarks, bringing machine action recognition closer to human-like robustness. Project page: $\href{https://www.vision.caltech.edu/actiondiff/}{\texttt{vision.caltech.edu/actiondiff}}$ Code: $\href{https://github.com/frankyaoxiao/ActionDiff}{\texttt{github.com/frankyaoxiao/ActionDiff}}$
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, focusing on the requested elements:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ActionDiff, a novel approach to action recognition that leverages features extracted from pre-trained Stable Video Diffusion (SVD) models.  The core idea is that these diffusion-based features, particularly from earlier timesteps in the denoising process, encode high-level semantic information that is robust to domain shifts. The authors show that ActionDiff achieves state-of-the-art generalization performance across several challenging scenarios: recognizing actions performed by different animal species, adapting to varying viewpoints (first-person vs. third-person), and dealing with different recording contexts (e.g., sports vs. movies). The approach utilizes a frozen SVD backbone with a learned transformer to aggregate the features. The authors systematically compare diffusion-derived features against other self-supervised backbones, demonstrating the superior semantic richness of diffusion features and their ability to generalize across diverse domains. They also explore the impact of conditioning the diffusion model on earlier timesteps, confirming that these features are more robust to domain shift.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the application of video diffusion model features, especially focusing on earlier timesteps' semantics, to domain generalization in action recognition.  While diffusion models have been used for perception tasks before, their role in high-level video understanding and the careful analysis of timestep conditioning are relatively unexplored. Leveraging SVD architecture, while powerful, it reduces the novelty aspect somewhat. The insight that features from earlier diffusion timesteps emphasize semantic information over pixel-level details is significant. However, the architecture itself, using a transformer on top of pre-trained features, is not particularly groundbreaking.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a crucial limitation of current action recognition systems: their lack of robustness to domain shifts. The ability to generalize across species, viewpoints, and contexts is essential for deploying action recognition models in real-world applications. The demonstrated improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods on challenging benchmarks are compelling. The investigation into which layers and timesteps are most useful for generalization provides valuable insights for future research in the area. The paper demonstrates the potential of diffusion models to overcome the lack of generalization in action recognition systems.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on multiple domain generalization benchmarks. The performance gains over existing methods are significant.
    * **Thorough Analysis:**  The authors conduct a systematic analysis of the impact of different factors, such as timestep conditioning, layer selection, and backbone choice.
    * **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of domain shift in action recognition and motivates the use of diffusion models as a potential solution.
    * **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with a clear explanation of the method and experimental setup.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational Cost:**  The paper acknowledges the high computational cost of diffusion-based feature extraction, which could limit its practical applicability.  While acknowledging this, the paper provides no concrete solutions or paths for mitigation.
    * **Architectural Novelty:** The overall architecture, while effective, relies heavily on a pre-trained model and uses a relatively standard transformer-based classification head. The main contribution hinges on *feature engineering* rather than architectural innovation.
    * **Limited Ablation:** While there is an ablation study, it could have been more in-depth.  For instance, exploring different training strategies for the transformer head or fine-tuning a small portion of the SVD model could provide further insights. The authors use a smaller SVD model in ablations. This impacts the credibility of the ablation studies as the findings are dependent on the model size.
    * **Dependence on SVD architecture:** The success is tightly coupled with SVD architecture. It is unclear if the gains are purely from the SVD model's pretraining or specific architectural components of diffusion models.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research into the use of diffusion models for high-level video understanding tasks. It may influence the development of more robust and generalizable action recognition systems. The findings regarding the importance of semantic features from earlier diffusion timesteps could inform the design of future feature extraction methods. However, the computational cost and reliance on very large pre-trained models might limit its immediate adoption in resource-constrained settings.

* **Score Rationale:** Given the significance of the problem addressed, the strong empirical results, and insightful analysis, but acknowledging the lack of significant architectural innovation, the heavy dependence on a pre-trained model (which limits accessibility), and the high computational cost, the paper merits a score of 7. The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating the effectiveness of diffusion features for domain generalization in action recognition and by providing insights into their properties, but the high cost and limited architectural novelty prevent it from reaching a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PromptGuard: An Orchestrated Prompting Framework for Principled Synthetic Text Generation for Vulnerable Populations using LLMs with Enhanced Safety, Fairness, and Controllability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08910v1)
- **Authors**: Tung Vu, Lam Nguyen, Quynh Dao
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of Large Language Models (LLMs) in real-world applications poses unprecedented risks of generating harmful, biased, or misleading information to vulnerable populations including LGBTQ+ individuals, single parents, and marginalized communities. While existing safety approaches rely on post-hoc filtering or generic alignment techniques, they fail to proactively prevent harmful outputs at the generation source. This paper introduces PromptGuard, a novel modular prompting framework with our breakthrough contribution: VulnGuard Prompt, a hybrid technique that prevents harmful information generation using real-world data-driven contrastive learning. VulnGuard integrates few-shot examples from curated GitHub repositories, ethical chain-of-thought reasoning, and adaptive role-prompting to create population-specific protective barriers. Our framework employs theoretical multi-objective optimization with formal proofs demonstrating 25-30% analytical harm reduction through entropy bounds and Pareto optimality. PromptGuard orchestrates six core modules: Input Classification, VulnGuard Prompting, Ethical Principles Integration, External Tool Interaction, Output Validation, and User-System Interaction, creating an intelligent expert system for real-time harm prevention. We provide comprehensive mathematical formalization including convergence proofs, vulnerability analysis using information theory, and theoretical validation framework using GitHub-sourced datasets, establishing mathematical foundations for systematic empirical research.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper and a rigorous critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PromptGuard, a framework designed to mitigate harmful or biased outputs from Large Language Models (LLMs) when interacting with vulnerable populations. A key component, VulnGuard Prompt, uses contrastive learning based on real-world data from GitHub, ethical chain-of-thought reasoning, and adaptive role-prompting to build population-specific protections. The framework employs modular design, multi-objective optimization with theoretical guarantees of harm reduction, and mathematical formalization including convergence proofs and vulnerability analysis. It aims to be an intelligent expert system for real-time harm prevention.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a crucial problem: preventing LLMs from generating harmful outputs targeting vulnerable populations. The idea of proactively addressing this at the prompt level ("PromptGuard") is promising. The core innovation, VulnGuard Prompt, leverages several techniques (contrastive learning, few-shot examples, ethical reasoning, role-playing) to create a protective layer. The emphasis on mathematical formalization, convergence proofs, and theoretical harm reduction is also a strength, suggesting a rigorous approach. The use of real-world data from GitHub is also a positive, grounding the approach in practical, observed vulnerabilities.

However, several aspects warrant critical scrutiny:

*   **Reliance on GitHub data:** While using real-world data is good, GitHub content can also be biased or contain harmful examples. The paper needs to address how it filters and validates the GitHub data to ensure it contributes to *reducing* harm rather than perpetuating it. Simply sourcing data from GitHub isn't inherently virtuous; the quality and curation of that data are crucial.
*   **Scope of "Vulnerable Populations":** The paper mentions LGBTQ+ individuals, single parents, and marginalized communities. However, vulnerabilities are often highly intersectional and context-dependent. The paper needs to clearly define the specific characteristics of these populations it targets and acknowledge the limitations of a one-size-fits-all approach within these groups. The "population-specific protective barriers" should be better explained with concrete examples.
*   **"Ethical Chain-of-Thought Reasoning":** The paper needs to elaborate on the practical implementation of this. How is "ethical reasoning" encoded and applied within the prompt? What ethical frameworks are considered, and how are potential conflicts between different ethical frameworks resolved?
*   **Overly Optimistic Claims:** Phrases like "25-30% analytical harm reduction" and "intelligent expert system" should be tempered with realistic caveats. Without strong empirical validation, such claims can be misleading. The theoretical guarantees are valuable, but they are contingent on assumptions that may not hold in real-world applications.
*   **Lack of Concrete Examples:** The paper describes the framework at a high level but lacks detailed examples of how the prompts are constructed, how contrastive learning is applied, and how the modules interact in practice. Showing a few examples of input prompts, the resulting VulnGuard Prompt, and the model's response would significantly strengthen the paper.
*   **Evaluation Methodology:** The paper mentions a "theoretical validation framework using GitHub-sourced datasets." This isn't a substitute for empirical evaluation with real users and diverse scenarios. The paper needs to explicitly state how the framework will be rigorously tested and evaluated in practice. It needs to define concrete metrics to measure the success of preventing harm. The current description focuses more on theoretical underpinnings, leaving the evaluation section underdeveloped. How well does this system perform under adversarial attacks? How does it compare to other state-of-the-art safety techniques?
*   **Scalability:** Complex prompting strategies can be computationally expensive. The paper needs to discuss the computational overhead of PromptGuard and its scalability to large-scale deployments.
*   **Maintainability:** How easy is it to update and maintain the VulnGuard Prompt as LLMs evolve and new vulnerabilities emerge? The framework needs to be adaptable to changing contexts and threats.

Overall, the paper presents a potentially valuable approach to a critical problem. However, the current presentation relies too heavily on theoretical formalization and lacks sufficient concrete examples, empirical validation, and detailed discussion of the practical challenges. The claims of harm reduction are premature without robust experimental evidence. The reliance on GitHub data needs a more critical and cautious approach.

Score: 6

Rationale: The paper addresses a vital problem and presents a promising conceptual framework. The mathematical formalization is commendable. However, the lack of concrete examples, a comprehensive evaluation plan, critical discussion of GitHub data limitations, and overly optimistic claims significantly detract from its current impact. It demonstrates potential but requires substantial further work to demonstrate its true value and effectiveness. Without more rigorous empirical validation and practical implementation details, the impact on the field remains uncertain.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Towards Trustworthy AI: Characterizing User-Reported Risks across LLMs "In the Wild"
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08912v1)
- **Authors**: Lingyao Li, Renkai Ma, Zhaoqian Xue, Junjie Xiong
- **Abstract**: While Large Language Models (LLMs) are rapidly integrating into daily life, research on their risks often remains lab-based and disconnected from the problems users encounter "in the wild." While recent HCI research has begun to explore these user-facing risks, it typically concentrates on a singular LLM chatbot like ChatGPT or an isolated risk like privacy. To gain a holistic understanding of multi-risk across LLM chatbots, we analyze online discussions on Reddit around seven major LLM chatbots through the U.S. NIST's AI Risk Management Framework. We find that user-reported risks are unevenly distributed and platform-specific. While "Valid and Reliable" risk is the most frequently mentioned, each product also exhibits a unique "risk fingerprint;" for instance, user discussions associate GPT more with "Safe" and "Fair" issues, Gemini with "Privacy," and Claude with "Secure and Resilient" risks. Furthermore, the nature of these risks differs by their prevalence: less frequent risks like "Explainability" and "Privacy" manifest as nuanced user trade-offs, more common ones like "Fairness" are experienced as direct personal harms. Our findings reveal gaps between risks reported by system-centered studies and by users, highlighting the need for user-centered approaches that support users in their daily use of LLM chatbots.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary of the paper, followed by a critical evaluation and scoring:

**Summary:**

The paper "Towards Trustworthy AI: Characterizing User-Reported Risks across LLMs “In the Wild" investigates user-perceived risks associated with Large Language Models (LLMs) by analyzing Reddit discussions. The authors use the NIST AI Risk Management Framework (ARF) as a lens to categorize these risks across seven major LLM chatbots (ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, DeepSeek, Llama, Mistral, and Qwen). They find that user-reported risks are unevenly distributed, platform-specific, and that the nature of these risks varies based on their prevalence (trade-offs vs. direct harms). The research identifies a disconnect between system-centered and user-centered risk perspectives and discusses the trade-offs users make to navigate these risks, offering design and policy implications for user-centered AI risk management.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary strength lies in its user-centered approach to AI risk assessment.  While system-centered analyses are common, directly mining user-generated content from Reddit to understand *how* users experience risks "in the wild" is a valuable contribution. The multi-LLM chatbot comparison is also a novel aspect, distinguishing it from prior work that primarily focused on ChatGPT or a single risk. The use of NIST’s ARF as a structured way to categorize and then connect these identified risks from a user-centered perspective is methodologically sound, and represents a thoughtful way to approach the analysis. However, while the combination of methods is valuable, the individual methods themselves are reasonably standard.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant for several reasons.  First, it highlights the limitations of purely technical or lab-based risk mitigation. By demonstrating the gap between system-defined and user-experienced risks, it underscores the need for user-centered approaches. The identification of specific "risk fingerprints" for different LLMs is valuable for developers. The paper's findings provide practical implications for design (e.g., meta-interfaces for comparing LLM responses) and policy (e.g., decoupling essential features from privacy-invasive practices). It also contributes to ongoing discussions about the "privacy paradox" and the ethical considerations of AI transparency.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **User-Centric Focus:** Addresses a critical gap in current AI risk research.
    *   **Multi-LLM Perspective:** Moves beyond single-LLM case studies, providing a broader understanding of risk patterns.
    *   **Methodological Rigor:** Employs a mixed-methods approach, combining quantitative analysis (topic modeling, statistical testing) with qualitative analysis of user experiences. The inter-coder reliability in the top-down analysis lends credibility to the automated risk extraction process.
    *   **Practical Implications:** Offers concrete design and policy recommendations for mitigating user-perceived risks.
    *   **Open Science:** Makes the interactive Knowledge Graph publicly accessible, enabling further exploration and analysis.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Reddit Data:** Limits generalizability due to the demographic biases of Reddit users. The choice of subreddits (though broad) could also influence the types of risks identified.
    *   **Potential for Annotation Bias:** While inter-coder reliability is reported for the ground truth dataset, the LLM-based annotation pipeline could still introduce biases. The potential for topic modeling to group risks based on keywords rather than true thematic similarity needs to be considered.
    *   **Limited Depth of Qualitative Analysis:**  While the paper presents illustrative quotes, a deeper qualitative analysis (e.g., thematic analysis, narrative analysis) could provide richer insights into the user experience of risk.  Paraphrasing for anonymity, while ethically sound, could also obscure important nuances.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to other Risk Frameworks:** The paper focuses on the NIST ARF. While valid, including other risk frameworks would have strengthened the paper by expanding the scope of the analysis.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence both AI development practices and policy. By highlighting the importance of user-centered risk assessment, it could encourage developers to adopt more human-centered design approaches. The paper's findings could inform policy discussions about AI transparency, accountability, and safety. It also adds to the broader HCI literature on trust, privacy, and ethical AI.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable user-centered perspective on AI risk and provides empirical evidence to support its claims. While the limitations of using Reddit data and the potential for annotation bias are acknowledged, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses.  The multi-LLM comparison and the identification of trade-offs users make in navigating risks are particularly insightful. Although the methodological contributions are incremental, the application of these methods to address a critical gap in the AI risk literature warrants a high rating.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CY
- **Score**: 7/10

### Documents Are People and Words Are Items: A Psychometric Approach to Textual Data with Contextual Embeddings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08920v1)
- **Authors**: Jinsong Chen
- **Abstract**: This research introduces a novel psychometric method for analyzing textual data using large language models. By leveraging contextual embeddings to create contextual scores, we transform textual data into response data suitable for psychometric analysis. Treating documents as individuals and words as items, this approach provides a natural psychometric interpretation under the assumption that certain keywords, whose contextual meanings vary significantly across documents, can effectively differentiate documents within a corpus. The modeling process comprises two stages: obtaining contextual scores and performing psychometric analysis. In the first stage, we utilize natural language processing techniques and encoder based transformer models to identify common keywords and generate contextual scores. In the second stage, we employ various types of factor analysis, including exploratory and bifactor models, to extract and define latent factors, determine factor correlations, and identify the most significant words associated with each factor. Applied to the Wiki STEM corpus, our experimental results demonstrate the method's potential to uncover latent knowledge dimensions and patterns within textual data. This approach not only enhances the psychometric analysis of textual data but also holds promise for applications in fields rich in textual information, such as education, psychology, and law.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a new method for analyzing textual data by combining large language models (LLMs) and psychometric techniques. It transforms text into a format suitable for psychometric analysis by using contextual embeddings from LLMs to generate contextual scores for keywords within documents. Documents are treated as individuals, words as items, and factor analysis is performed to uncover latent dimensions and patterns within the text corpus. The method is applied to the Wiki STEM corpus and shows potential for extracting meaningful insights from textual data in various fields.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a potentially valuable approach to analyzing textual data, but its novelty and significance need careful consideration.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Combination:** The primary strength lies in the integration of LLMs and psychometrics. While both fields are well-established, their combination for text analysis is relatively novel. It leverages the power of LLMs to capture contextual nuance, which is a significant improvement over traditional text analysis methods like simple keyword counts or bag-of-words models.
*   **Psychometric Rigor:** Applying psychometric techniques like factor analysis provides a structured and statistically grounded way to uncover latent dimensions. This allows for more robust and interpretable results compared to purely qualitative analysis or unsupervised learning techniques that may not provide such rigorous justification.
*   **Potential Applications:** The method holds promise for various applications, including understanding educational content, analyzing psychological texts, and extracting knowledge from legal documents. The wide applicability increases the potential impact.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper presents a relatively clear two-stage process that is easy to understand. This facilitates replication and further development by other researchers.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Keyword Selection:** The approach heavily relies on the initial selection of keywords. The choice of these keywords can significantly influence the resulting factors and their interpretation. The paper needs to rigorously address the sensitivity of the method to keyword selection and how to ensure representativeness and minimize bias in the selection process. A discussion on how to avoid "cherry-picking" keywords to support pre-existing hypotheses is crucial.
*   **"Individuals" and "Items" Analogy:** While framing documents as individuals and words as items is a clever analogy, the validity of this analogy needs further justification. The underlying assumptions of psychometric models, such as the independence of responses, might not directly translate to textual data. For example, the "response" (contextual score) of a word in one document is likely not independent of the other words in that document. The paper should acknowledge and discuss these limitations.
*   **Lack of Comparison to Existing Methods:** The paper would be strengthened by a more thorough comparison to existing text analysis techniques. How does this approach perform compared to topic modeling (e.g., LDA, NMF), semantic network analysis, or other embedding-based methods? A clear demonstration of the advantages of the proposed method over established techniques is essential for establishing its novelty and practical utility.
*   **Limited Validation:** The application to the Wiki STEM corpus is a good starting point, but the paper needs more rigorous validation. How well do the uncovered "latent knowledge dimensions" align with expert knowledge? Are the factors replicable across different datasets or with different keyword sets? More thorough validation is needed to demonstrate the robustness and reliability of the method.
*   **Interpretability of Contextual Scores:** The contextual scores derived from LLMs can be complex and difficult to interpret. The paper should provide more guidance on how to interpret these scores and how they relate to the underlying semantic meaning of the words. Without clear interpretability, the factor analysis results might be difficult to translate into actionable insights.
*  **Computational Cost:** LLMs can be computationally expensive to run, especially on large datasets. The paper could discuss the computational feasibility of their method, especially compared to traditional psychometric methods, and ways to mitigate this cost.

**Novelty and Significance:**

While the combination of LLMs and psychometrics is a promising direction, the novelty is somewhat incremental. Individual components (LLMs, factor analysis) are well-established. The value comes from the application of one tool on data prepared by the other. The significance hinges on whether the proposed method can provide unique insights that are not easily obtainable through other techniques. This is currently not convincingly demonstrated.

**Score:**

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and interesting approach with the potential to improve text analysis, but it suffers from several limitations that need to be addressed. The combination of LLMs and psychometrics is a strength, but the assumptions underlying the analogy between documents/words and individuals/items require further justification. The lack of comparison to existing methods and the limited validation weaken the claims of novelty and significance. While the paper offers a promising framework, more work is needed to establish its practical utility and impact. A slightly above average score reflects the potential coupled with the current limitations. Addressing the weaknesses regarding keyword sensitivity, validation, interpretability, and comparison to existing methods could significantly improve the paper's impact and justify a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Deploying AI for Signal Processing education: Selected challenges and intriguing opportunities
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08950v1)
- **Authors**: Jarvis Haupt, Qin Lu, Yanning Shen, Jia Chen, Yue Dong, Dan McCreary, Mehmet Akçakaya, Georgios B. Giannakis
- **Abstract**: Powerful artificial intelligence (AI) tools that have emerged in recent years -- including large language models, automated coding assistants, and advanced image and speech generation technologies -- are the result of monumental human achievements. These breakthroughs reflect mastery across multiple technical disciplines and the resolution of significant technological challenges. However, some of the most profound challenges may still lie ahead. These challenges are not purely technical but pertain to the fair and responsible use of AI in ways that genuinely improve the global human condition. This article explores one promising application aligned with that vision: the use of AI tools to facilitate and enhance education, with a specific focus on signal processing (SP). It presents two interrelated perspectives: identifying and addressing technical limitations, and applying AI tools in practice to improve educational experiences. Primers are provided on several core technical issues that arise when using AI in educational settings, including how to ensure fairness and inclusivity, handle hallucinated outputs, and achieve efficient use of resources. These and other considerations -- such as transparency, explainability, and trustworthiness -- are illustrated through the development of an immersive, structured, and reliable "smart textbook." The article serves as a resource for researchers and educators seeking to advance AI's role in engineering education.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper:

The paper discusses the potential of AI to enhance education, specifically in signal processing (SP). It addresses the technical challenges (fairness, hallucination, resource efficiency) associated with using AI in education and proposes a framework for developing a "smart textbook" that is immersive, structured, and reliable, while emphasizing transparency, explainability, and trustworthiness. The paper aims to guide researchers and educators in leveraging AI to improve engineering education.

Now, here's a rigorous and critical evaluation of the paper's novelty and significance:

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its holistic approach to applying AI in a specific educational context (signal processing) while explicitly addressing and attempting to mitigate key ethical and practical challenges. While using AI in education isn't entirely new, and neither are the individual challenges it discusses (fairness, hallucinations), the paper's contribution is in framing them within the specific context of a "smart textbook" and offering a unified perspective for researchers and educators. The idea of creating an immersive, structured, and reliable learning environment powered by AI is a positive step. However, the abstract suggests a conceptual framework rather than presenting concrete implementations or experimental results. This reduces the novelty somewhat.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its focus on responsible AI deployment in education. By highlighting fairness, explainability, and other crucial aspects, it contributes to the ongoing conversation about ethical AI and encourages a more thoughtful approach to its integration in educational settings. The focus on signal processing, a fundamental yet challenging subject in engineering, makes the paper particularly relevant to engineering education. The "smart textbook" concept, if properly developed, could significantly improve student learning outcomes and engagement. The article aims to fill the gap in the field by providing a resource that integrates both the technical and ethical concerns, paving a way to develop AI-based educational resources. However, the true significance will depend on the specifics of the "smart textbook" implementation, which is not detailed in the provided abstract.

**Strengths:**

*   **Holistic approach:** Considers both technical and ethical aspects of AI in education.
*   **Focus on a specific subject:** Signal processing provides a concrete context for discussion.
*   **Addresses key challenges:** Fairness, hallucination, resource efficiency are important considerations.
*   **Aims to be a resource for researchers and educators:** Potential for practical impact.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of concrete details:** The abstract presents a conceptual framework rather than specific implementations or experimental results. This limits the immediate impact.
*   **"Smart textbook" concept needs elaboration:** The abstract doesn't provide sufficient detail about the proposed textbook's features, functionalities, or evaluation methods.
*   **General AI risks:** The paper mentions AI limitations like fairness and hallucination, but the degree to which these issues are new is questionable. Generative AI is not intrinsically any different than existing statistical models in this regard, making it unclear how impactful the claims may be.

**Justification:**

The paper's novelty is modest, as it combines existing concepts and concerns within a specific application context. Its significance is higher due to its emphasis on responsible AI and its potential to guide future research and development efforts. However, the lack of concrete details and the dependence on future implementation to realize its potential limits its current impact. Considering these factors, a score of 6 seems appropriate. It acknowledges the paper's valuable contribution to the discourse on ethical AI in education and its potential for future impact while recognizing its limitations in terms of concrete novelty and immediate practical implications.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: eess.SP
- **Score**: 6/10

### CoSwin: Convolution Enhanced Hierarchical Shifted Window Attention For Small-Scale Vision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08959v1)
- **Authors**: Puskal Khadka, Rodrigue Rizk, Longwei Wang, KC Santosh
- **Abstract**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) have achieved impressive results in computer vision by leveraging self-attention to model long-range dependencies. However, their emphasis on global context often comes at the expense of local feature extraction in small datasets, particularly due to the lack of key inductive biases such as locality and translation equivariance. To mitigate this, we propose CoSwin, a novel feature-fusion architecture that augments the hierarchical shifted window attention with localized convolutional feature learning. Specifically, CoSwin integrates a learnable local feature enhancement module into each attention block, enabling the model to simultaneously capture fine-grained spatial details and global semantic structure. We evaluate CoSwin on multiple image classification benchmarks including CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, MNIST, SVHN, and Tiny ImageNet. Our experimental results show consistent performance gains over state-of-the-art convolutional and transformer-based models. Notably, CoSwin achieves improvements of 2.17% on CIFAR-10, 4.92% on CIFAR-100, 0.10% on MNIST, 0.26% on SVHN, and 4.47% on Tiny ImageNet over the baseline Swin Transformer. These improvements underscore the effectiveness of local-global feature fusion in enhancing the generalization and robustness of transformers for small-scale vision. Code and pretrained weights available at https://github.com/puskal-khadka/coswin
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CoSwin, a novel Vision Transformer (ViT) architecture designed to improve performance on image classification tasks, especially with smaller datasets. CoSwin augments the Swin Transformer with a learnable local feature enhancement module within each attention block. This module aims to capture fine-grained spatial details, addressing a weakness of standard ViTs that often prioritize global context at the expense of local feature extraction. Experimental results on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, MNIST, SVHN, and Tiny ImageNet demonstrate consistent performance improvements over the baseline Swin Transformer and other state-of-the-art models. The paper highlights the effectiveness of fusing local convolutional features with global attention mechanisms to enhance generalization and robustness, especially for small-scale vision.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *specific way* CoSwin integrates local feature enhancement into the Swin Transformer architecture. The idea of combining convolutional and attention-based mechanisms isn't entirely new (e.g., earlier hybrid approaches exist), but the *implementation* through a learnable local feature enhancement module within *each* Swin Transformer block is a distinct contribution. The "Co-" in CoSwin seemingly alludes to the cooperative learning of attention and convolution. While not revolutionary, the paper carves out a niche by focusing on a specific integration strategy within a very popular transformer architecture (Swin) to address a well-known problem (ViTs lacking inductive bias for small datasets). The learnable aspect adds a layer of adaptability compared to hard-coded convolutional modules, but the exact architecture of the enhancement module is not deeply explored in the abstract. This leaves the reader wanting to know more about the implementation details of this enhancement module.

**Significance:** The significance hinges on the demonstrated performance gains, particularly on smaller datasets like CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet. The improvements over the Swin Transformer baseline are meaningful and suggest that the proposed local-global feature fusion is effective. The importance increases with the current community interest on transformer models. The release of code and pretrained weights is also a positive factor, enabling further research and adoption by the community. If this paper has a big impact, it will be because of its practical applicability. I anticipate this paper will be influential if the code is easy to use and readily adaptable to new tasks. A weakness of the paper is that the results are for smaller datasets and would greatly benefit from results on ImageNet and or other large datasets.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation of ViTs on small datasets.
*   **Well-Defined Solution:** The proposed CoSwin architecture is well-articulated, even in the abstract.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides empirical evidence of performance improvements on multiple datasets.
*   **Reproducibility:** Code and pretrained weights are publicly available.
*   **Relatively Simple Design:** The described architecture is fairly simple and the modularity of the enhancement module within each Swin Transformer block promotes adaptability.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the *specific implementation* is novel, the general concept of combining convolutional and attention mechanisms is not entirely new. The novelty is the specific integration strategy.
*   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The abstract does not mention ablation studies to clearly show the contribution of the local feature enhancement module. Without it, it is difficult to determine if the whole is greater than the sum of its parts.
*   **Limited Dataset Variety:** The results primarily focus on smaller datasets. Testing on larger, more complex datasets (e.g., ImageNet) would significantly strengthen the paper.
*   **Unclear Architecture Details:** The abstract does not cover implementation details of the enhancement module, specifically about its architecture.
*   **Lack of comparison with other hybrid methods:** The abstract would benefit from highlighting the advantages over other works.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence research in the following areas:

*   **Vision Transformer Architectures:** Other researchers may build upon the CoSwin architecture to further improve performance on small datasets.
*   **Hybrid Convolutional-Attention Models:** The paper could inspire new methods for integrating convolutional and attention mechanisms.
*   **Inductive Biases for ViTs:** The work highlights the importance of incorporating inductive biases into ViTs to improve generalization.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution with a clearly defined problem, a well-articulated solution, and supporting empirical evidence. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, as it builds upon existing work in hybrid convolutional-attention models. While the performance gains are significant on the tested datasets, the lack of results on larger datasets and detailed ablation studies limits the paper's overall impact. The relatively simple design and availability of code/weights are positive factors. Overall, the paper demonstrates a useful technique for improving ViT performance on small datasets, but it does not represent a groundbreaking breakthrough. The score reflects the incremental novelty combined with the significant empirical improvements, offset by the lack of ablation studies and evaluation on larger datasets.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### BRoverbs -- Measuring how much LLMs understand Portuguese proverbs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08960v1)
- **Authors**: Thales Sales Almeida, Giovana Kerche Bonás, João Guilherme Alves Santos
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) exhibit significant performance variations depending on the linguistic and cultural context in which they are applied. This disparity signals the necessity of mature evaluation frameworks that can assess their capabilities in specific regional settings. In the case of Portuguese, existing evaluations remain limited, often relying on translated datasets that may not fully capture linguistic nuances or cultural references. Meanwhile, native Portuguese-language datasets predominantly focus on structured national exams or sentiment analysis of social media interactions, leaving gaps in evaluating broader linguistic understanding. To address this limitation, we introduce BRoverbs, a dataset specifically designed to assess LLM performance through Brazilian proverbs. Proverbs serve as a rich linguistic resource, encapsulating cultural wisdom, figurative expressions, and complex syntactic structures that challenge the model comprehension of regional expressions. BRoverbs aims to provide a new evaluation tool for Portuguese-language LLMs, contributing to advancing regionally informed benchmarking. The benchmark is available at https://huggingface.co/datasets/Tropic-AI/BRoverbs.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper:

The paper introduces BRoverbs, a novel dataset designed to evaluate the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in understanding Brazilian Portuguese, focusing specifically on proverbs. Existing Portuguese LLM evaluations are limited, often relying on translated datasets or focusing on narrow domains like national exams or social media sentiment. BRoverbs addresses this gap by leveraging proverbs' rich linguistic and cultural context, offering a more challenging and nuanced benchmark for assessing LLMs' ability to comprehend regional expressions and cultural wisdom. The dataset is publicly available on Hugging Face.

Now, a rigorous and critical evaluation:

This paper addresses a real and recognized problem: the lack of adequate evaluation datasets for Portuguese-language LLMs, particularly those capturing the nuances of Brazilian Portuguese. The current landscape is indeed dominated by translated datasets or datasets focusing on very specific tasks that don't adequately stress the comprehension abilities of LLMs regarding cultural understanding and figurative language.

**Strengths:**

*   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper directly tackles a deficiency in the evaluation landscape for Portuguese LLMs. The focus on proverbs is a clever and potentially impactful choice. Proverbs are dense, culturally specific, and often rely on figurative language, making them a strong test of understanding.
*   **Novelty:** While datasets exist for sentiment analysis and general tasks in Portuguese, the specific focus on proverbs as an evaluation tool appears novel. The choice to focus on *Brazilian* Portuguese further refines the contribution and targets a specific regional dialect.
*   **Public Availability:** Making the dataset publicly available on Hugging Face is crucial for its adoption and impact. This allows other researchers to easily use and build upon the work.
*   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing evaluation methods and justifies the need for BRoverbs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Evaluation Methodology:** The paper lacks details about the specific tasks used to evaluate the LLMs with BRoverbs. Is it a multiple-choice task? Is it a translation task? Is it a proverb completion task? The paper needs to elaborate on how the dataset is *intended* to be used in evaluation. Without this detail, it's difficult to assess the potential impact of the dataset.
*   **Dataset Size and Composition:** The paper doesn't provide sufficient information about the size of the dataset (number of proverbs), the source of the proverbs (e.g., collected from books, online sources, etc.), or any potential biases within the dataset. A detailed breakdown of the dataset's content is crucial.
*   **Baseline Performance:** The paper does not present baseline results using BRoverbs with existing LLMs. Showing how some baseline models perform (even poorly) helps to demonstrate the difficulty of the task and the value of the dataset.
*   **Limited Discussion of Related Work:** While the paper mentions the limitations of existing datasets, it could benefit from a more thorough discussion of related work in cross-lingual transfer learning, cultural adaptation of LLMs, and existing resources for Portuguese language processing (beyond just datasets).
*   **Potential for ambiguity:** Proverbs can have multiple interpretations or contexts, which may complicate the evaluation process. This aspect should be addressed in the paper, possibly through annotator guidelines or specific metrics that account for semantic variation.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly contribute to the evaluation of Portuguese LLMs, particularly in capturing cultural nuances. However, the impact depends heavily on the quality and thoroughness of the dataset itself, as well as the practicality of the evaluation framework built around it. The lack of details on the dataset's composition, evaluation methodology, and baseline results weakens the overall impact.

**Justification for the score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-motivated idea. Addressing the gap in Portuguese LLM evaluation with a focus on proverbs is a smart approach. However, the lack of crucial details regarding the dataset's composition, evaluation methodology, and the absence of baseline results significantly limits its immediate impact. The paper is a good starting point but needs significant expansion and validation to fully realize its potential. It promises novelty and potentially high impact, but it hasn't fully delivered it yet.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### FoundationalECGNet: A Lightweight Foundational Model for ECG-based Multitask Cardiac Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.08961v1)
- **Authors**: Md. Sajeebul Islam Sk., Md Jobayer, Md Mehedi Hasan Shawon, Md. Golam Raibul Alam
- **Abstract**: Cardiovascular diseases (CVDs) remain a leading cause of mortality worldwide, underscoring the importance of accurate and scalable diagnostic systems. Electrocardiogram (ECG) analysis is central to detecting cardiac abnormalities, yet challenges such as noise, class imbalance, and dataset heterogeneity limit current methods. To address these issues, we propose FoundationalECGNet, a foundational framework for automated ECG classification. The model integrates a dual-stage denoising by Morlet and Daubechies wavelets transformation, Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM), Graph Attention Networks (GAT), and Time Series Transformers (TST) to jointly capture spatial and temporal dependencies in multi-channel ECG signals. FoundationalECGNet first distinguishes between Normal and Abnormal ECG signals, and then classifies the Abnormal signals into one of five cardiac conditions: Arrhythmias, Conduction Disorders, Myocardial Infarction, QT Abnormalities, or Hypertrophy. Across multiple datasets, the model achieves a 99% F1-score for Normal vs. Abnormal classification and shows state-of-the-art performance in multi-class disease detection, including a 99% F1-score for Conduction Disorders and Hypertrophy, as well as a 98.9% F1-score for Arrhythmias. Additionally, the model provides risk level estimations to facilitate clinical decision-making. In conclusion, FoundationalECGNet represents a scalable, interpretable, and generalizable solution for automated ECG analysis, with the potential to improve diagnostic precision and patient outcomes in healthcare settings. We'll share the code after acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FoundationalECGNet, a novel deep learning framework for automated ECG classification. It addresses challenges like noise, class imbalance, and data heterogeneity in ECG analysis by employing a dual-stage wavelet denoising technique, Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM), Graph Attention Networks (GAT), and Time Series Transformers (TST). FoundationalECGNet performs a two-stage classification: first distinguishing between Normal and Abnormal ECGs, then classifying Abnormal ECGs into five specific conditions (Arrhythmias, Conduction Disorders, Myocardial Infarction, QT Abnormalities, or Hypertrophy). The model achieves high F1-scores across multiple datasets, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance, and provides risk level estimations to aid clinical decision-making.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

This paper presents a reasonably novel architecture for ECG classification, combining several established techniques in a potentially effective way. The two-stage denoising with Morlet and Daubechies wavelets could be a significant contribution, if demonstrated rigorously, by mitigating noise-related challenges in ECG signals. Similarly, the fusion of CBAM, GAT, and TST to capture both spatial and temporal dependencies in ECG data shows promise for improved feature extraction.

However, several aspects warrant critical examination.

*   **Incremental Innovation:** The individual components (wavelet denoising, CBAM, GAT, TST) are not novel in themselves. The *combination* is the primary novel aspect. Therefore, the paper's novelty hinges on the specific architecture, training methodology, and the extent to which it outperforms existing methods. The claim of "state-of-the-art performance" needs extremely strong validation against a broad range of benchmarks and datasets.
