However, the incremental algorithmic novelty and the limited scope of the experiments prevent it from achieving a higher score. If it were compared to SOTA and the benefits highlighted and better understood, the score would have been higher.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TigerCoder: A Novel Suite of LLMs for Code Generation in Bangla
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09101v1)
- **Authors**: Nishat Raihan, Antonios Anastasopoulos, Marcos Zampieri
- **Abstract**: Despite being the 5th most spoken language, Bangla remains underrepresented in Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly for code generation. This primarily stems from the scarcity of high-quality data to pre-train and/or finetune such models. Hence, we introduce the first dedicated family of Code LLMs for Bangla (1B & 9B). We offer three major contributions: (1) a comprehensive Bangla code instruction datasets for programming domain adaptation; (2) MBPP-Bangla, an evaluation benchmark for Bangla code generation; and (3) the TigerCoder-family of Code LLMs, achieving significant ~11-18% performance gains at Pass@1 over existing multilingual and general-purpose Bangla LLMs. Our findings show that curated, high-quality datasets can overcome limitations of smaller models for low-resource languages. We open-source all resources to advance further Bangla LLM research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the underrepresentation of Bangla in code generation LLMs due to a lack of high-quality training data. The authors introduce TigerCoder, a family of code LLMs specifically for Bangla (1B and 9B parameters). Their contributions include: (1) a new Bangla code instruction dataset for programming domain adaptation; (2) MBPP-Bangla, a benchmark for evaluating Bangla code generation; and (3) the TigerCoder models themselves, which outperform existing multilingual and general-purpose Bangla LLMs by 11-18% in Pass@1. The paper highlights the importance of curated datasets in improving the performance of smaller models for low-resource languages and open-sources all resources to facilitate further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

This paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of multilingual and low-resource LLMs, specifically focusing on code generation for Bangla.  Let's break down the evaluation:

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a Clear Gap:** The paper clearly identifies and addresses a significant gap: the underrepresentation of Bangla in code generation LLMs. This is a relevant and timely problem, especially given the growing importance of AI accessibility in diverse linguistic contexts.
*   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The creation of a dedicated Bangla code instruction dataset is a significant contribution. Data scarcity is a major bottleneck for low-resource languages, and this dataset helps overcome that challenge. The quality and scale of the dataset are crucial, although the paper doesn't provide extensive details on the dataset creation process (which is a slight weakness discussed below).
*   **Evaluation Benchmark (MBPP-Bangla):**  Developing an evaluation benchmark, MBPP-Bangla, is essential for objectively assessing the performance of Bangla code generation models. This allows for standardized comparisons and facilitates future research.
*   **Performance Improvement:** Achieving 11-18% performance gains (Pass@1) over existing models is a substantial improvement, demonstrating the effectiveness of their approach. This shows that specialized training is beneficial for low-resource languages.
*   **Open-Sourcing:**  Open-sourcing the dataset, benchmark, and models is a major strength. This promotes reproducibility, encourages further research, and benefits the broader community.
*   **Focus on Practicality:** The work explicitly aims to provide usable Code LLMs for Bangla, emphasizing their real-world application rather than pure theoretical novelty.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dataset Details:** The paper could benefit from providing more detailed information on the dataset creation process. What are the sources of the Bangla code? How was the data cleaned and preprocessed? What are the statistics of the dataset (e.g., number of code snippets, average length)? Without this information, it's difficult to fully assess the quality and representativeness of the dataset. Were translation techniques employed, and if so, what measures were taken to ensure high-quality translations? Details regarding the training data's characteristics and the curation process are important for assessing reliability and reproducibility.
*   **Model Architecture Details:** While the authors present the models, they could provide more detail about the architectures used as well as the pre-training process. What base models were used for fine-tuning? What are the specific hyperparameters used during training? This information would enable other researchers to replicate and build upon their work.
*   **Limited Baselines:** While the paper demonstrates improvements over existing multilingual and general-purpose Bangla LLMs, a comparison against other techniques for low-resource language adaptation could be valuable. For instance, exploring methods like cross-lingual transfer learning (without fine-tuning) would provide a clearer picture of the benefits of the proposed approach.
*   **Generalizability:** While the paper focuses on Bangla, the core idea of creating high-quality instruction datasets for low-resource languages is applicable to other languages. The paper could discuss the generalizability of their approach and the challenges involved in applying it to other languages.
*   **Error Analysis:** A qualitative error analysis of the generated code could provide insights into the limitations of the models and guide future research directions. For example, what types of coding errors are common? Are there specific programming concepts that the models struggle with?
*   **Computational resources:** It would be beneficial to document the amount of computational resources required to train these models. Knowing the scale of the computation needed helps other researchers reproduce and potentially scale-up the models further.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies in the specific application of code LLMs to Bangla and the creation of resources tailored for this low-resource language. While the underlying techniques (fine-tuning, instruction-following) are not entirely new, the *application* to Bangla code generation and the creation of the dataset and benchmark represent a valuable and novel contribution. The significance stems from the potential to democratize AI development for Bangla speakers and enable them to leverage code generation tools in their native language.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7.5**. The paper addresses a clear need, provides valuable resources (dataset, benchmark, models), and demonstrates significant performance improvements. However, the lack of detailed information on the dataset creation, model architecture, and comprehensive error analysis limits the paper's impact and potential for further research. The absence of a more rigorous evaluation with other relevant baselines and the lack of a clear discussion on the generalizability of the approach slightly detract from the paper's contribution. Nevertheless, the practical impact and open-sourcing of resources are substantial and warrant a strong positive rating.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Character-Level Perturbations Disrupt LLM Watermarks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09112v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaoxi Zhang, Xiaomei Zhang, Yanjun Zhang, He Zhang, Shirui Pan, Bo Liu, Asif Qumer Gill, Leo Yu Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Model (LLM) watermarking embeds detectable signals into generated text for copyright protection, misuse prevention, and content detection. While prior studies evaluate robustness using watermark removal attacks, these methods are often suboptimal, creating the misconception that effective removal requires large perturbations or powerful adversaries. To bridge the gap, we first formalize the system model for LLM watermark, and characterize two realistic threat models constrained on limited access to the watermark detector. We then analyze how different types of perturbation vary in their attack range, i.e., the number of tokens they can affect with a single edit. We observe that character-level perturbations (e.g., typos, swaps, deletions, homoglyphs) can influence multiple tokens simultaneously by disrupting the tokenization process. We demonstrate that character-level perturbations are significantly more effective for watermark removal under the most restrictive threat model. We further propose guided removal attacks based on the Genetic Algorithm (GA) that uses a reference detector for optimization. Under a practical threat model with limited black-box queries to the watermark detector, our method demonstrates strong removal performance. Experiments confirm the superiority of character-level perturbations and the effectiveness of the GA in removing watermarks under realistic constraints. Additionally, we argue there is an adversarial dilemma when considering potential defenses: any fixed defense can be bypassed by a suitable perturbation strategy. Motivated by this principle, we propose an adaptive compound character-level attack. Experimental results show that this approach can effectively defeat the defenses. Our findings highlight significant vulnerabilities in existing LLM watermark schemes and underline the urgency for the development of new robust mechanisms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the robustness of LLM watermarks against removal attacks. The key insight is that character-level perturbations, which disrupt tokenization, are more effective than token-level or sentence-level modifications for removing watermarks, even under constrained editing budgets.  The paper formalizes the watermark removal problem, defines realistic threat models with limited detector access, and proposes a Genetic Algorithm (GA)-based removal attack guided by a reference detector to overcome the limitations of black-box settings. Finally, it addresses defense mechanisms with an adaptive compound character-level attack, demonstrating vulnerabilities of existing LLM watermark schemes and underscoring the need for robust designs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of LLM watermarking by highlighting the vulnerabilities of current schemes against character-level perturbation attacks, a weakness often overlooked in prior research. The formalization of the watermark removal problem and the definition of realistic threat models are also strengths, providing a more practical evaluation framework. The proposal of a GA-based guided removal attack is a sound approach given the limitations of black-box access to the watermark detector.

However, several aspects warrant critical consideration:

*   **Novelty of Character-Level Attacks:** While the effectiveness of character-level perturbations *in this specific context* of LLM watermarks might be novel, the concept itself isn't groundbreaking. Character-level and subword-level attacks have been widely explored in adversarial NLP literature (e.g. against text classifiers and machine translation) for years. The paper could improve by more explicitly acknowledging this existing body of work and differentiating its specific application to LLM watermark removal, emphasizing how disrupting tokenization changes the game specifically for watermarks.
*   **Significance of the Reference Detector:** The effectiveness of the GA depends heavily on the reference detector's quality.  The paper demonstrates this, but the limitations of this approach (sensitivity to hyperparameters, imperfect approximation of the real detector) should be emphasized even more. The discussion about its shortcomings in Appendix A-E is crucial, but this awareness needs greater prominence in the main body. Gradient-based methods failing due to "mismatch" is a key finding.
*   **Adaptive Attack is Incremental, Not Radical:** The adaptive attack strategy is conceptually sound (addressing the adversarial dilemma). However, the specific implementation seems like a incremental improvement rather than a transformative solution. The use of compound perturbations is somewhat intuitive. The core idea of proactively adapting to the defense should be highlighted.
*   **Limited Defenses:** The defense strategy that the adaptive attack is targeting is very limited and simple, only consisting of spell check, OCR etc. Thus, while it is effective at bypassing these defenses, its robustness against more sophisticated defenses remains uncertain.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a solid technical contribution and has practical relevance given the growing interest in LLM watermarking. The weaknesses mentioned above prevent a higher score, as they impact the magnitude of the novelty and the generalizability of the findings. While the character-level attack concept is not entirely new, its effective exploitation within the specific context of LLM watermarks, alongside the realistic threat models and GA-based optimization, constitutes a valuable contribution. It also clearly demonstrate vulnerabilities and highlight the urgency for development of new robust mechansisms to improve robusteness in future.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Sensitivity-LoRA: Low-Load Sensitivity-Based Fine-Tuning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09119v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Zhang, Bo Huang, Zhenjia Li, Xi Xiao, Hui Yi Leong, Zumeng Zhang, Xinwei Long, Tianyang Wang, Hao Xu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have transformed both everyday life and scientific research. However, adapting LLMs from general-purpose models to specialized tasks remains challenging, particularly in resource-constrained environments. Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), a prominent method within Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT), has emerged as a promising approach to LLMs by approximating model weight updates using low-rank decomposition. However, LoRA is limited by its uniform rank ( r ) allocation to each incremental matrix, and existing rank allocation techniques aimed at addressing this issue remain computationally inefficient, complex, and unstable, hindering practical applications. To address these limitations, we propose Sensitivity-LoRA, an efficient fine-tuning method that dynamically allocates ranks to weight matrices based on both their global and local sensitivities. It leverages the second-order derivatives (Hessian Matrix) of the loss function to effectively capture weight sensitivity, enabling optimal rank allocation with minimal computational overhead. Our experimental results have demonstrated robust effectiveness, efficiency and stability of Sensitivity-LoRA across diverse tasks and benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the Sensitivity-LoRA paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Sensitivity-LoRA, a parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) method for large language models (LLMs).  It improves upon LoRA by dynamically allocating rank to weight matrices based on their global and local sensitivities, estimated using second-order derivatives (Hessian matrix). This dynamic allocation aims to address the limitation of LoRA's uniform rank assignment, where each incremental matrix is allocated a fixed rank, irrespective of its importance. The method leverages Hessian-based metrics, including the trace of the Hessian, Topk, and Effective Rank, to capture weight sensitivity. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness, efficiency, and stability of Sensitivity-LoRA across diverse tasks and benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically allocating ranks in LoRA based on sensitivity metrics isn't entirely new. Prior works such as AdaLoRA and DyLoRA have explored dynamic rank allocation. However, Sensitivity-LoRA attempts to improve upon existing methods by using a Hessian-based sensitivity metric, offering a different approach for determining rank allocation. The use of both global and local sensitivity metrics is a reasonable addition and potentially useful for capturing different aspects of weight importance.

**Significance:** The paper claims to offer a more efficient and stable method for dynamic rank allocation. If the method demonstrates a significant performance boost compared to existing techniques while maintaining or improving efficiency, it could be a valuable contribution to the PEFT field. The experiments, therefore, become crucial for validating these claims.

**Strengths:**

*   **Hessian-based sensitivity:** The use of the Hessian matrix for sensitivity estimation is a theoretically sound approach, as it provides information about the curvature of the loss landscape and weight importance.
*   **Global and Local sensitivity:** Integration of both global and local sensitivities provides a more comprehensive view of weight importance, which leads to better tuning.
*   **Comprehensive experimental evaluation:** The paper showcases extensive experimental evaluation across NLU and NLG tasks and various datasets. This provides a solid foundation for evaluating performance and efficiency. The ablation study contributes to the assessment of how each component influences the overall result. The experiments demonstrate that the combination of global and local sensitivity provides the best results, showing that combining multiple sources of data helps in accurate decision making.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational cost of Hessian calculation:** Calculating the full Hessian matrix for large models is computationally expensive. While the paper mentions approximations (row-wise computation with activations or cholesky decomposition), the details and effectiveness of these approximations need further elaboration. The comparison of overhead cost should be made with other rank allocation methods to understand the advantages of this method
*   **Marginal improvements:** The experimental results show performance improvements compared to LoRA and other baselines, but it is questionable whether this is a marginal improvement considering that all methods achieve a higher accuracy. The actual difference between the approach and other baselines isn't significant when compared to their complexities. The paper should be compared with SVD methods (Zhang et al., 2023c; Hu et al., 2023; Zhang et al., 2023b) for efficiency and effectiveness.
*   **Lack of theoretical analysis:** The paper could benefit from a more in-depth theoretical analysis of the proposed method.

**Potential Influence:**

Sensitivity-LoRA has the potential to influence the field of parameter-efficient fine-tuning by providing a new method for dynamic rank allocation and its effectiveness in different tasks. The thorough investigation into sensitivity metrics could promote better performance and stability in training different models. The main thing that impacts its actual usage is the overhead of using Hessian approximations.

**Justification:**

The assigned score acknowledges the paper's potential contribution in refining dynamic rank allocation through a specific sensitivity estimation approach, while also accounting for limitations such as the computational complexity of Hessian calculations and marginal performance gains. The comprehensive experimental evaluation provides solid support for the findings, but the limitations in theoretical analysis and experimental insights reduce the impact.
The novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking, which results in lower rating.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Compass-v3: Scaling Domain-Specific LLMs for Multilingual E-Commerce in Southeast Asia
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09121v1)
- **Authors**: Sophia Maria
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) excel in general-domain applications, yet their performance often degrades in specialized tasks requiring domain-specific knowledge. E-commerce is particularly challenging, as its data are noisy, heterogeneous, multilingual, and highly dynamic. We present Compass-v3, a vertical-domain Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) model with 245B total parameters and 71B active per token, designed for Southeast Asian e-commerce. Compass-v3 adopts fewer but larger experts, combined with hardware-efficient optimizations-such as intra-node expert parallelism and a customized memcpy operator-to maximize GPU utilization. The model is trained on 12T tokens of curated multilingual corpora and large-scale synthetic e-commerce instructions using a mixed-training strategy. To enhance alignment, we propose Optimal-Transport Direct Preference Optimization (OTPO), which captures token-level distinctions and improves instruction adherence in commerce-specific scenarios. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that Compass-v3 delivers state-of-the-art e-commerce performance, surpassing DeepSeek-V3.1, GPT-4 series, and Qwen3-235B. Moreover, Compass-v3 demonstrates strong multilingual capability across low-resource Southeast Asian languages (Indonesian, Thai, Filipino, Vietnamese, Malay, Taglog) and Portuguese while sustaining competitive performance on general benchmarks. It has already been widely applied in Shopee's industrial-scale e-commerce platform and is gradually replacing OpenAI's traffic, now accounting for over 70\% of total LLM usage, highlighting its dual strengths in specialized commerce expertise and broad linguistic competence.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Compass-v3, a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) language model specifically designed for Southeast Asian (SEA) e-commerce. The model features 245B parameters (71B active), a smaller number of larger experts than typical MoE models, hardware-aware optimizations (expert parallelism, optimized memcpy), and is trained on 12T tokens of multilingual and synthetic e-commerce data. The authors also introduce Optimal-Transport Direct Preference Optimization (OTPO) for improved alignment and demonstrate that Compass-v3 achieves state-of-the-art results in e-commerce, multilingual tasks, and general benchmarks compared to models like GPT-4, DeepSeek, and Qwen. The paper highlights its successful deployment within Shopee's e-commerce platform.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the combination of several aspects.
    *   The paper builds upon previous work on Compass, therefore some elements are iterative.
    *   The specific *architecture* (fewer but larger experts with intra-node parallelism) and *training optimizations* are relatively novel in the MoE context, targeting specific GPU resource constraints.  While MoE is not new, specific implementations and adaptations for a specific domain (e-commerce) and resource availability do present a novel angle.
    *   The *data curation and synthetic data generation* pipeline focused on SEA e-commerce is unique.  Creating high-quality multilingual e-commerce datasets for low-resource languages in Southeast Asia is a significant contribution in itself.
    *   *OTPO is potentially novel,* claiming token-level differentiation in preference pairs. It would be crucial to examine the claimed novelty with more detail.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper tackles a practical and important problem: scaling LLMs for real-world e-commerce in a diverse multilingual region.
    *   The results presented are strong, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance in various tasks and languages. It validates the effectiveness of the proposed techniques.
    *   The real-world deployment in Shopee is compelling and supports the claims of practical utility.  It shows that their model is not just theoretically good, but also provides value in a production setting.
    *   The model could contribute to improved user experiences, increased sales, and more efficient operations on the Shopee platform.
    *   The focus on resource-efficient training and inference optimizations are important for wider adoption, especially in regions where compute resources are limited.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive evaluation covering e-commerce, multilingual, and general benchmarks.
    *   Detailed description of the architecture, training process, and optimization techniques.
    *   Clear presentation of results with comparative analysis.
    *   Demonstrated practical impact in a real-world e-commerce platform.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper provides a decent level of technical detail, it would benefit from more in-depth ablation studies for some of the introduced techniques (e.g., individual contribution of each optimization). More quantitative analysis on the efficacy of OTPO is crucial.
    *   Lack of detailed comparative benchmarks directly comparing resource usage with baseline models. Including metrics like training time and inference cost will further enhance the claims of resource efficiency.
    *   The exact composition and quality metrics of the synthetic e-commerce data could be described more explicitly. There is less information on the creation of negative samples which contribute to learning a good reward model.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of domain-specific LLMs, particularly in resource-constrained settings. The techniques and insights described can be valuable for researchers and practitioners working on similar problems.  It can also serve as a template for building and deploying similar models in other multilingual and specialized domains.
* **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper warrants a score that reflects its novel contributions in architectural design, the construction of high-quality, SEA-specific datasets, and the performance gains achieved on real-world tasks. However, its lack of detailed ablation studies and limited benchmarking against established resource consumption metrics prevent it from achieving a higher score. Furthermore, the novelty and analysis of OTPO are under-detailed.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### ALL-PET: A Low-resource and Low-shot PET Foundation Model in the Projection Domain
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09130v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Huang, Kang Chen, Bingxuan Li, Huafeng Liu, Qiegen Liu
- **Abstract**: Building large-scale foundation model for PET imaging is hindered by limited access to labeled data and insufficient computational resources. To overcome data scarcity and efficiency limitations, we propose ALL-PET, a low-resource, low-shot PET foundation model operating directly in the projection domain. ALL-PET leverages a latent diffusion model (LDM) with three key innovations. First, we design a Radon mask augmentation strategy (RMAS) that generates over 200,000 structurally diverse training samples by projecting randomized image-domain masks into sinogram space, significantly improving generalization with minimal data. This is extended by a dynamic multi-mask (DMM) mechanism that varies mask quantity and distribution, enhancing data diversity without added model complexity. Second, we implement positive/negative mask constraints to embed strict geometric consistency, reducing parameter burden while preserving generation quality. Third, we introduce transparent medical attention (TMA), a parameter-free, geometry-driven mechanism that enhances lesion-related regions in raw projection data. Lesion-focused attention maps are derived from coarse segmentation, covering both hypermetabolic and hypometabolic areas, and projected into sinogram space for physically consistent guidance. The system supports clinician-defined ROI adjustments, ensuring flexible, interpretable, and task-adaptive emphasis aligned with PET acquisition physics. Experimental results show ALL-PET achieves high-quality sinogram generation using only 500 samples, with performance comparable to models trained on larger datasets. ALL-PET generalizes across tasks including low-dose reconstruction, attenuation correction, delayed-frame prediction, and tracer separation, operating efficiently with memory use under 24GB.
- **Summary**: ## Concise Summary of the Paper

The paper introduces ALL-PET, a low-resource PET foundation model designed to address data scarcity and computational limitations in PET imaging. ALL-PET leverages a latent diffusion model (LDM) in the projection domain and incorporates three key innovations: (1) Radon Mask Augmentation Strategy (RMAS) and Dynamic Multi-Mask (DMM) for generating diverse training samples; (2) positive/negative mask constraints to enforce geometric consistency; and (3) Transparent Medical Attention (TMA) for enhancing lesion-related regions in the sinogram. Experiments demonstrate that ALL-PET can generate high-quality sinograms with limited data (500 samples) and generalizes across various PET imaging tasks, including low-dose reconstruction, attenuation correction, delayed-frame prediction, and tracer separation, with low memory usage.

## Rigorous and Critical Evaluation

This paper presents a compelling approach to building a foundation model for PET imaging, particularly addressing the significant challenges of data scarcity and computational resources. The core strength lies in its creative use of the projection domain and the development of tailored techniques to leverage limited data effectively.

**Strengths:**

* **Addressing a Key Problem:** The paper directly tackles the major bottleneck of data scarcity in medical imaging, which significantly hinders the development and deployment of powerful deep learning models. Focusing on the projection domain (sinogram) is smart, as it allows for physics-informed augmentations that are more challenging and relevant than simply augmenting image-domain data.
* **Novel Augmentation Strategies (RMAS & DMM):** The Radon Mask Augmentation Strategy (RMAS) is a genuinely innovative way to generate a large amount of diverse training data from a limited set of real images. By projecting randomized masks into sinogram space, the method creates samples that are inherently consistent with the physics of PET imaging. The Dynamic Multi-Mask (DMM) further enhances data diversity without increasing model complexity. This approach is a significant improvement over traditional data augmentation techniques.
* **Geometric Constraints:** Incorporating positive/negative mask constraints to enforce geometric consistency is another strong point. This regularization strategy not only improves generation quality but also reduces the parameter burden of the model, making it more efficient.
* **Transparent Medical Attention (TMA):** TMA is a valuable contribution because it allows for the incorporation of prior knowledge (coarse segmentation) in a physically consistent and interpretable manner. The ability to adjust ROI based on clinician input adds a layer of flexibility and control, making the model more clinically relevant. This addresses a common critique of deep learning models in medical imaging - the lack of interpretability and control.
* **Generalizability:** The demonstrated generalizability across multiple tasks (low-dose reconstruction, attenuation correction, delayed-frame prediction, and tracer separation) is impressive and highlights the potential of ALL-PET as a foundation model.
* **Low Resource Requirements:** The claim of low memory usage (under 24GB) is critical for enabling wider adoption of this technology in clinical settings where resources may be limited.

**Weaknesses:**

* **Limited Quantitative Evaluation:** While the paper claims performance comparable to models trained on larger datasets, the quantitative evaluation appears to be somewhat limited. The paper would benefit from more rigorous quantitative comparisons against state-of-the-art methods on each of the evaluated tasks. Specific metrics and statistical significance need to be clearly presented.
* **Dependency on Coarse Segmentation:** The TMA module relies on coarse segmentation for lesion-focused attention. While the authors state that the model supports hypermetabolic and hypometabolic areas, the accuracy and reliability of the final ALL-PET model directly depend on the quality of the initial coarse segmentation. The paper should address this limitation more directly and explore the sensitivity of the model to variations in the segmentation. What happens if the initial segmentation is inaccurate?
* **Lack of Ablation Studies for DMM:** Although RMAS and mask constraints are discussed, it's not clear if the DMM mechanism significantly improves upon using a fixed set of masks. Ablation studies that isolate the effect of DMM would strengthen the paper.
* **Limited Dataset Details:** While the paper mentions using 500 samples, more details about the composition and characteristics of these samples would be helpful. What pathologies were included? Were the samples representative of the clinical population? This impacts the generalizability of the conclusions.
* **Lack of Comparative Analysis with alternative low-resource methods:** The paper presents a strong approach but doesn't thoroughly compare with alternative low-resource or few-shot learning techniques in medical imaging. A discussion and comparison with methods like meta-learning, transfer learning from simulated data, or self-supervised pre-training on unlabeled data would add more context and strengthen the contribution.

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

Despite the identified weaknesses, the paper's strengths outweigh its limitations. ALL-PET represents a significant step towards building practical foundation models for PET imaging in resource-constrained environments. The proposed techniques, particularly RMAS, DMM, and TMA, are novel and well-suited to the specific challenges of PET imaging. The demonstrated generalizability across multiple tasks further enhances the potential impact of this work. If the authors can address the weaknesses outlined above, particularly by providing more robust quantitative evaluation and a more thorough comparison to alternative approaches, the paper will be even more impactful. The potential to facilitate wider adoption of advanced PET imaging analysis and reduce the cost of developing new PET applications is significant.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and valuable approach to addressing data scarcity in PET imaging. The creative use of projection-domain augmentations and geometric constraints is a significant strength. The demonstrated generalizability across multiple tasks and low resource requirements make ALL-PET a promising technology. However, the limited quantitative evaluation, dependency on coarse segmentation, and lack of comparative analysis with alternative low-resource methods hold the paper back from a higher score. Addressing these weaknesses would significantly enhance the impact and influence of this work.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adaptive Pareto-Optimal Token Merging for Edge Transformer Models in Semantic Communication
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09168v1)
- **Authors**: Omar Erak, Omar Alhussein, Hatem Abou-Zeid, Mehdi Bennis
- **Abstract**: Large-scale transformer models have emerged as a powerful tool for semantic communication systems, enabling edge devices to extract rich representations for robust inference across noisy wireless channels. However, their substantial computational demands remain a major barrier to practical deployment in resource-constrained 6G networks. In this paper, we present a training-free framework for adaptive token merging in pretrained vision transformers to jointly reduce inference time and transmission resource usage. We formulate the selection of per-layer merging proportions as a multi-objective optimization problem to balance accuracy and computational cost. We employ Gaussian process-based Bayesian optimization to construct a Pareto frontier of optimal configurations, enabling flexible runtime adaptation to dynamic application requirements and channel conditions. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms other baselines and achieves significant reductions in floating-point operations while maintaining competitive accuracy across a wide range of signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) conditions. Additional results highlight the effectiveness of adaptive policies that adjust merging aggressiveness in response to channel quality, providing a practical mechanism to trade off latency and semantic fidelity on demand. These findings establish a scalable and efficient approach for deploying transformer-based semantic communication in future edge intelligence systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper proposes a training-free framework for optimizing the deployment of large vision transformers in semantic communication systems for resource-constrained 6G networks. The method uses adaptive token merging to reduce computational load and transmission resource usage without retraining. It formulates the token merging proportion selection as a multi-objective optimization problem, balancing accuracy and computational cost. Gaussian process-based Bayesian optimization is used to find Pareto-optimal configurations for different application and channel conditions. The results demonstrate significant reduction in computational cost and transmission overhead, while maintaining accuracy and adapting to varying channel quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant and important problem: deploying computationally expensive vision transformers in resource-constrained edge environments for semantic communication. This is particularly crucial for future 6G networks where edge intelligence will play a significant role. The proposed approach of training-free adaptive token merging is a clever way to address the computational burden without incurring the expense of retraining or fine-tuning models for specific channel conditions or resource constraints.

Here's a breakdown of the strengths and weaknesses, contributing to the overall score:

**Strengths:**

*   **Relevance:** The problem addressed is highly relevant to the emerging field of semantic communication and edge intelligence in 6G networks.
*   **Novelty:** While token merging itself isn't entirely new, the application within a training-free, adaptive framework optimized for semantic communication is a novel contribution. Using Bayesian optimization to discover Pareto-optimal token merging configurations across transformer layers is also a good idea.
*   **Training-Free:** The training-free nature is a significant advantage, as it avoids the cost and complexity of retraining or fine-tuning, making the approach more practical for deployment in dynamic environments.
*   **Adaptive:** The adaptive policy based on channel quality demonstrates the practicality and flexibility of the proposed framework.
*   **Clear Formulation:** The problem is clearly formulated as a multi-objective optimization problem, and the use of Gaussian process-based Bayesian optimization is well-justified.
*   **Experimental Results:** The experiments appear to be thorough, comparing against relevant baselines and demonstrating performance across a range of SNRs. The claim of reducing floating point operations (FLOPs) while maintaining competitive accuracy is significant.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is novel, the individual components (token merging, Bayesian optimization) are well-established. The true *algorithmic* novelty might be somewhat limited.
*   **Baseline Comparison:** The paper needs to be carefully evaluated to assess the baselines it compares with. If the baselines are not state-of-the-art approaches for model compression or adaptive inference, the significance of the results is diminished. Are other, more recent compression techniques considered?
*   **Practical Deployment Considerations:** While the paper highlights adaptability, it could benefit from a more detailed discussion of practical deployment considerations. For instance, how often is the Bayesian optimization process executed? How is the current channel quality estimated in a real-world scenario? What is the overhead of the Bayesian Optimization procedure? Are there any computational limitations in using Bayesian optimization at the edge device itself?
*   **Generalizability:** The experimental evaluation focuses on vision transformers. While this is a common use case, the generalizability of the approach to other types of transformers (e.g., those used for natural language processing) should be discussed.
*   **Theoretical Guarantees:** While empirical results are presented, the paper lacks theoretical guarantees on the convergence or optimality of the Bayesian optimization process in this specific context. This would strengthen the contribution.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper addresses a relevant and important problem with a practical and novel approach. The use of a training-free adaptive framework for token merging, combined with Bayesian optimization, is a valuable contribution to the field of semantic communication. The experimental results support the claims of reducing computational cost and maintaining accuracy.

However, the individual components are not entirely novel, and there are some weaknesses regarding baseline comparisons, practical deployment considerations, generalizability, and theoretical guarantees. These weaknesses prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The strength is that the combination of the approach and its impact on semantic communication is where the core novelty lies.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### EchoX: Towards Mitigating Acoustic-Semantic Gap via Echo Training for Speech-to-Speech LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09174v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhao Zhang, Yuhao Du, Zhanchen Dai, Xiangnan Ma, Kaiqi Kou, Benyou Wang, Haizhou Li
- **Abstract**: Speech-to-speech large language models (SLLMs) are attracting increasing attention. Derived from text-based large language models (LLMs), SLLMs often exhibit degradation in knowledge and reasoning capabilities. We hypothesize that this limitation arises because current training paradigms for SLLMs fail to bridge the acoustic-semantic gap in the feature representation space. To address this issue, we propose EchoX, which leverages semantic representations and dynamically generates speech training targets. This approach integrates both acoustic and semantic learning, enabling EchoX to preserve strong reasoning abilities as a speech LLM. Experimental results demonstrate that EchoX, with about six thousand hours of training data, achieves advanced performance on multiple knowledge-based question-answering benchmarks. The project is available at https://github.com/FreedomIntelligence/EchoX.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EchoX: Towards Mitigating Acoustic-Semantic Gap via Echo Training for Speech-to-Speech LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EchoX, a novel training paradigm for speech-to-speech large language models (SLLMs).  The core hypothesis is that existing SLLMs suffer from an acoustic-semantic gap in the feature representation space, leading to degradation in knowledge and reasoning capabilities compared to text-based LLMs. EchoX addresses this gap by using a pre-trained text-to-codec model to dynamically generate speech training targets based on semantic representations, integrating acoustic and semantic learning.  The method consists of three stages: speech-to-text LLM training, text-to-codec training, and "Echo training," where the text-to-codec model decodes the outputs of the speech-to-text LLM.  The authors show that EchoX, trained on a smaller dataset, achieves competitive performance on knowledge-based question-answering benchmarks. They also use unit language tokens to significantly reduce sequence lengths and address the challenge of long-sequence generation by introducing a trigger for streaming generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of Echo training is somewhat novel. Using the T2C to generate targets and reduce the reliance on ground truth audio targets is an interesting way to bridge the acoustic semantic gap.  It does differ from existing works focused on bridging speech and text representations. The use of unit language and streaming trigger is not particularly groundbreaking, but sensible in the context of speech.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of this work lies in its ability to train high-performing SLLMs with less training data. If validated by the community, it could make SLLM development more accessible and efficient. Showing competitive performance on established QA benchmarks definitely helps its case. Its most significant limitation is the reliance on accurate pre-trained components such as the text-to-codec and the speech-to-text models, since errors generated by them would be propogated.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a well-defined problem in SLLMs: the acoustic-semantic gap.
    *   The Echo training methodology is intuitive and provides a clear mechanism for bridging the gap.
    *   The paper presents empirical results showing competitive performance compared to other SLLMs, despite using a significantly smaller training dataset.
    *   Uses unit language to decrease sequence length while maintaining decent speech quality.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper heavily relies on several pre-trained components (S2T model, Text-to-Codec, HiFi-GAN), and the performance of EchoX is thus constrained by these components. More detailed analysis of error propagation from these components could be added.
    *   Lack of ablations to justify the components being used, to determine if they are all necessary and critical. Specifically, the effect of Denoising Adapter should be ablated.
    *   Limited explanation of data cleaning and normalization strategies can make reproducibility difficult.
    *   The experimental setup is relatively constrained to knowledge-based QA. Testing on a wider range of tasks (e.g., speech translation, dialog) would strengthen the generalizability claims.
    *   The subjective testing is carried out with only 5 testers. A greater number is recommended.

*   **Potential Influence:** The EchoX approach could potentially influence future research directions in SLLM training, encouraging further exploration of techniques for aligning acoustic and semantic representations more effectively. It could also drive research into more efficient training methods that reduce reliance on massive datasets.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty of the Echo training approach, the significance of addressing the acoustic-semantic gap, and the competitive performance demonstrated in the experiments, this paper offers a valuable contribution. However, the heavy reliance on pre-trained components, limited experimental scope, and the relatively small subjective testing group restrict its impact. The paper provides a good starting point, but with future improvements it could offer considerable value.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### AI Reasoning for Wireless Communications and Networking: A Survey and Perspectives
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09193v1)
- **Authors**: Haoxiang Luo, Yu Yan, Yanhui Bian, Wenjiao Feng, Ruichen Zhang, Yinqiu Liu, Jiacheng Wang, Gang Sun, Dusit Niyato, Hongfang Yu, Abbas Jamalipour, Shiwen Mao
- **Abstract**: Artificial Intelligence (AI) techniques play a pivotal role in optimizing wireless communication networks. However, traditional deep learning approaches often act as closed boxes, lacking the structured reasoning abilities needed to tackle complex, multi-step decision problems. This survey provides a comprehensive review and outlook of reasoning-enabled AI in wireless communication networks, with a focus on Large Language Models (LLMs) and other advanced reasoning paradigms. In particular, LLM-based agents can combine reasoning with long-term planning, memory, tool utilization, and autonomous cross-layer control to dynamically optimize network operations with minimal human intervention. We begin by outlining the evolution of intelligent wireless networking and the limitations of conventional AI methods. We then introduce emerging AI reasoning techniques. Furthermore, we establish a classification system applicable to wireless network tasks. We also present a layer-by-layer examination for AI reasoning, covering the physical, data link, network, transport, and application layers. For each part, we identify key challenges and illustrate how AI reasoning methods can improve AI-based wireless communication performance. Finally, we discuss key research directions for AI reasoning toward future wireless communication networks. By combining insights from both communications and AI, this survey aims to chart a path for integrating reasoning techniques into the next-generation wireless networks.
- **Summary**: ## Summary:

This survey paper provides an overview of how reasoning-enabled AI, particularly using Large Language Models (LLMs) and other advanced reasoning paradigms, can optimize wireless communication networks. It argues that traditional deep learning lacks the structured reasoning necessary for complex, multi-step decision-making in this field. The paper outlines the evolution of intelligent wireless networking, limitations of existing AI methods, and introduces emerging AI reasoning techniques. It then classifies wireless network tasks and examines AI reasoning applications layer-by-layer (physical, data link, network, transport, application), highlighting challenges and potential improvements. Finally, it discusses future research directions for AI reasoning in next-generation wireless networks, aiming to bridge the gap between communications and AI.

## Critical Evaluation:

This paper tackles a crucial emerging area in wireless communication: the integration of advanced reasoning capabilities, especially using LLMs, into network optimization. While the overall direction is promising, the paper's actual novelty and impact need a critical examination.

**Strengths:**

* **Timeliness:** The topic is highly relevant. The intersection of LLMs and wireless communication is a hot area, and a survey consolidating the current state of knowledge is valuable.
* **Comprehensive Scope:** Covering all layers of the network stack provides a holistic view, which is a significant strength. This helps illustrate how reasoning can be applied across the entire network infrastructure.
* **Identifies Limitations of Existing AI:** The paper explicitly highlights the drawbacks of traditional "black-box" deep learning approaches, setting the stage for the need for reasoning-based methods.
* **Future Research Directions:** The discussion of future research avenues adds value by pointing readers toward promising areas for further investigation.

**Weaknesses:**

* **Limited Novelty in Content:** While timely, the paper's content largely summarizes existing research and proposes ideas based on those findings. It doesn't present fundamentally new architectures, algorithms, or theoretical contributions. It's a review and synthesis, not a creation of novel methods. It primarily presents existing research and potential applications of existing LLMs, rather than introducing novel approaches or architectures specifically designed for the wireless domain. This is a survey, which naturally has a lower threshold for novelty, but the assessment should still be rigorous.
* **Lack of Concrete Examples/Results:** The paper discusses potential improvements and applications, but it often lacks concrete examples or experimental results to substantiate the claims. The reader is left with hypothetical scenarios rather than tangible evidence of the efficacy of the proposed reasoning techniques.
* **Overselling of LLM Capabilities:** The paper risks overstating the current capabilities of LLMs. While LLMs show promise, they are not a panacea. Their application in the complex, real-time environment of wireless networks requires significant adaptation and faces challenges related to latency, computational cost, and the need for extensive domain-specific knowledge. The paper could benefit from a more nuanced discussion of these challenges.
* **Generic Treatment of Reasoning Techniques:** The discussion of "reasoning techniques" sometimes feels generic. More specific detail on how different reasoning paradigms (e.g., symbolic reasoning, probabilistic reasoning, neuro-symbolic methods) can be implemented and compared in different wireless network scenarios would strengthen the paper. The classification system, while useful, could be made more refined by providing clear application guidelines.
* **Limited Depth:** While covering all network layers provides breadth, it also results in a lack of depth in any specific area. The paper could have benefited from focusing on a smaller subset of layers and delving deeper into the challenges and opportunities within those layers.
* **Dependency on External LLMs:** The paper largely depends on the advancement of external LLMs, which makes the proposed techniques heavily reliant on the progress made in other research domains. The integration and tailoring of those external models for the wireless domain are often left ambiguous.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant in that it highlights a promising direction for future wireless network optimization. By advocating for reasoning-enabled AI, it encourages researchers to move beyond purely data-driven approaches and to consider incorporating more structured, knowledge-based methods. The paper's survey of existing literature and its identification of key challenges can serve as a valuable starting point for researchers entering this field. However, its impact will depend on how effectively it inspires and guides the development of concrete, practical solutions. The paper serves as a valuable signpost, highlighting the potential benefits and challenges of incorporating reasoning-enabled AI into wireless networks.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses discussed above, I believe a score of **6** is appropriate. The paper is timely and addresses a crucial emerging area. However, its lack of novelty in content, limited concrete examples, and tendency to overstate the current capabilities of LLMs prevent it from achieving a higher score. While it provides a valuable overview and identifies future research directions, its impact will depend on how well it inspires and guides the development of tangible solutions. The paper is a good survey, but not a groundbreaking contribution.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.NI
- **Score**: 6/10

### On Integrating Large Language Models and Scenario-Based Programming for Improving Software Reliability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09194v1)
- **Authors**: Ayelet Berzack, Guy Katz
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are fast becoming indispensable tools for software developers, assisting or even partnering with them in crafting complex programs. The advantages are evident -- LLMs can significantly reduce development time, generate well-organized and comprehensible code, and occasionally suggest innovative ideas that developers might not conceive on their own. However, despite their strengths, LLMs will often introduce significant errors and present incorrect code with persuasive confidence, potentially misleading developers into accepting flawed solutions. In order to bring LLMs into the software development cycle in a more reliable manner, we propose a methodology for combining them with ``traditional'' software engineering techniques in a structured way, with the goal of streamlining the development process, reducing errors, and enabling users to verify crucial program properties with increased confidence. Specifically, we focus on the Scenario-Based Programming (SBP) paradigm -- an event-driven, scenario-based approach for software engineering -- to allow human developers to pour their expert knowledge into the LLM, as well as to inspect and verify its outputs. To evaluate our methodology, we conducted a significant case study, and used it to design and implement the Connect4 game. By combining LLMs and SBP we were able to create a highly-capable agent, which could defeat various strong existing agents. Further, in some cases, we were able to formally verify the correctness of our agent. Finally, our experience reveals interesting insights regarding the ease-of-use of our proposed approach. The full code of our case-study will be made publicly available with the final version of this paper.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:**

The paper proposes a methodology for integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into the software development process more reliably by combining them with Scenario-Based Programming (SBP). This approach leverages human expertise within the SBP framework to guide and verify LLM-generated code, aiming to reduce errors and increase confidence in program correctness. The methodology is evaluated through a Connect4 case study, demonstrating the creation of a strong agent that can defeat existing agents and, in some cases, be formally verified. The paper concludes with insights into the usability of the proposed approach and promises to release the code for the case study.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

The paper addresses a timely and important problem: the unreliable nature of LLM-generated code and its integration into existing software development workflows. The core idea of combining LLMs with Scenario-Based Programming (SBP) is interesting and potentially valuable. SBP, with its focus on scenarios and event-driven architecture, provides a structured way to guide the LLM, incorporate human expertise, and verify outputs, mitigating the risks of blindly accepting LLM-generated code.

**Strengths:**

* **Addresses a relevant problem:** LLM integration into software development is a rapidly evolving field with significant challenges, and this paper directly tackles the issue of code reliability.
* **Novel combination of techniques:** Integrating LLMs with SBP is a non-trivial approach that could lead to more robust and verifiable software. The authors present a clear justification for choosing SBP and its benefits in the context of LLM-generated code.
* **Case study evaluation:** The Connect4 case study provides concrete evidence of the proposed methodology's effectiveness. The fact that the resulting agent can defeat strong existing agents is a notable achievement.
* **Verification potential:** The ability to formally verify parts of the agent provides compelling support for the methodology's ability to enhance code correctness.
* **Open source commitment:** Releasing the code for the case study significantly enhances the paper's impact and allows others to replicate and extend the work.

**Weaknesses:**

* **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is limited to a single case study (Connect4). While Connect4 is a challenging game, it is still a relatively constrained domain. The generalizability of the methodology to more complex and diverse software projects needs further investigation.
* **Lack of Specificity:** The paper describes a methodology, but it would be strengthened by more details on the *specific* techniques used to guide the LLM within the SBP framework. For instance, what kind of prompts were used? How was human expertise formalized within the scenarios? How were the LLM's outputs validated against the scenarios? More details would increase the reproducibility and usefulness of the work.
* **Limited Formal Verification:** The phrase "in some cases, we were able to formally verify the correctness of our agent" needs to be significantly clarified. What parts were verified? What verification techniques were used? How much of the overall system was covered by the formal verification? A more detailed explanation is necessary.
* **Scalability Concerns:** The effectiveness of SBP relies on the ability to identify and specify relevant scenarios. For significantly larger and more complex systems, creating and managing a comprehensive set of scenarios might become intractable. The paper doesn't explicitly address these scalability concerns.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and timely approach to integrating LLMs into software development by combining them with SBP. The Connect4 case study provides promising initial results, demonstrating the potential for creating strong agents and enabling formal verification. However, the paper's scope is limited, lacks sufficient detail on the specific techniques used, and doesn't fully address scalability concerns. While the open-source commitment enhances its potential impact, the current limitations prevent it from being considered a groundbreaking contribution. The combination of novel idea, promising results, and areas for improvement merit a score above average, but the need for more thorough evaluation and scalability analysis keeps it from scoring much higher.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enabling Regulatory Multi-Agent Collaboration: Architecture, Challenges, and Solutions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09215v1)
- **Authors**: Qinnan Hu, Yuntao Wang, Yuan Gao, Zhou Su, Linkang Du
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs)-empowered autonomous agents are transforming both digital and physical environments by enabling adaptive, multi-agent collaboration. While these agents offer significant opportunities across domains such as finance, healthcare, and smart manufacturing, their unpredictable behaviors and heterogeneous capabilities pose substantial governance and accountability challenges. In this paper, we propose a blockchain-enabled layered architecture for regulatory agent collaboration, comprising an agent layer, a blockchain data layer, and a regulatory application layer. Within this framework, we design three key modules: (i) an agent behavior tracing and arbitration module for automated accountability, (ii) a dynamic reputation evaluation module for trust assessment in collaborative scenarios, and (iii) a malicious behavior forecasting module for early detection of adversarial activities. Our approach establishes a systematic foundation for trustworthy, resilient, and scalable regulatory mechanisms in large-scale agent ecosystems. Finally, we discuss the future research directions for blockchain-enabled regulatory frameworks in multi-agent systems.
- **Summary**: ## Summary

This paper proposes a blockchain-enabled architecture to regulate the collaborative behavior of LLM-powered autonomous agents in multi-agent systems. The architecture consists of an agent layer, a blockchain data layer, and a regulatory application layer. It features three key modules: agent behavior tracing and arbitration, dynamic reputation evaluation, and malicious behavior forecasting. The goal is to establish a trustworthy, resilient, and scalable regulatory framework for these agents, enabling accountability, trust assessment, and early detection of adversarial activities. Finally, the paper discusses future research directions in this area.

## Critical Evaluation

This paper tackles a relevant and increasingly important problem: how to regulate the behavior of LLM-powered autonomous agents, particularly in collaborative settings. The use of blockchain as an underlying technology for transparency and accountability is a logical choice, given its inherent properties of immutability and distributed consensus. The proposed layered architecture is also sensible, separating the concerns of the agents themselves, the data related to their interactions, and the regulatory applications that analyze and enforce rules.

However, the paper's true novelty lies in the specific design and implementation details of the three key modules. Without a more detailed explanation of these modules and potentially some preliminary experimental results, it's difficult to assess their true effectiveness and innovation. For example:

*   **Agent behavior tracing and arbitration:** The paper mentions automated accountability, but doesn't delve into how agent behavior is traced and translated into verifiable claims on the blockchain. What specific aspects of agent behavior are tracked? How are conflicts or disagreements resolved? The arbitration process is crucial, and its design significantly impacts the system's fairness and effectiveness. Without these details, the module remains a high-level concept.
*   **Dynamic reputation evaluation:** The concept of dynamic reputation evaluation is not new in multi-agent systems. The novelty here would depend on how the authors adapt it to the specific challenges of LLM-powered agents, particularly their evolving capabilities and potential for deception. What specific metrics are used to evaluate reputation? How is reputation updated in real-time? How is the provenance of reputation data secured on the blockchain?
*   **Malicious behavior forecasting:** This is arguably the most challenging module. Predicting adversarial behavior of LLMs is a complex problem, and it's unclear how the authors plan to achieve this. What features are used to detect malicious patterns? Is it based on anomaly detection, supervised learning, or some other technique? The paper needs to provide more detail on the methodology and the expected accuracy of the forecasting model.

Furthermore, the paper lacks a clear discussion of the challenges associated with scaling this architecture to a large number of agents. Blockchain transactions can be expensive and slow, which could become a bottleneck in a large-scale system. The paper should address the potential performance limitations and discuss potential solutions, such as sharding or layer-2 scaling techniques.

The potential influence of the paper lies in its vision for a regulatory framework for LLM-powered agents. If the proposed modules are implemented effectively, they could provide a valuable tool for ensuring the responsible and beneficial use of these agents. However, the current lack of detail and experimental validation limits its immediate impact.

Despite these criticisms, the paper addresses a timely and relevant problem, and the proposed architecture offers a promising direction for future research. However, the lack of detail and the absence of experimental results prevent it from being considered a truly groundbreaking contribution. A more concrete implementation and evaluation would significantly enhance the paper's impact.

Score: 6

**Justification:**

The paper scores a 6 because it presents a reasonable and timely architecture using blockchain to regulate LLM-powered autonomous agents. While the overall concept is good, the lack of detail on the three key modules and the absence of experimental results significantly limit its novelty and immediate impact. The core ideas – tracing behavior, evaluating reputation, and forecasting malicious activity – are not entirely novel in themselves, and the paper needs to demonstrate how they are specifically adapted and implemented in the context of LLM agents using blockchain. The failure to address scaling issues also weakens the proposition. Thus, the paper presents a promising framework, but needs more substance and validation to be considered a significantly impactful contribution to the field. It's more of a promising direction than a solved problem at this stage.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Reading Between the Lines: Classifying Resume Seniority with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09229v1)
- **Authors**: Matan Cohen, Shira Shani, Eden Menahem, Yehudit Aperstein, Alexander Apartsin
- **Abstract**: Accurately assessing candidate seniority from resumes is a critical yet challenging task, complicated by the prevalence of overstated experience and ambiguous self-presentation. In this study, we investigate the effectiveness of large language models (LLMs), including fine-tuned BERT architectures, for automating seniority classification in resumes. To rigorously evaluate model performance, we introduce a hybrid dataset comprising both real-world resumes and synthetically generated hard examples designed to simulate exaggerated qualifications and understated seniority. Using the dataset, we evaluate the performance of Large Language Models in detecting subtle linguistic cues associated with seniority inflation and implicit expertise. Our findings highlight promising directions for enhancing AI-driven candidate evaluation systems and mitigating bias introduced by self-promotional language. The dataset is available for the research community at https://bit.ly/4mcTovt
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper and a critical evaluation of its novelty and significance:

**Summary:**

The paper explores using Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly fine-tuned BERT models, to automate the task of assessing candidate seniority from resumes. It addresses the challenge of inflated experience claims by introducing a hybrid dataset of real and synthetically generated resumes, including "hard examples" designed to simulate exaggerations and understatements. The research evaluates the LLMs' ability to detect subtle linguistic cues indicative of true seniority versus promotional language. The findings aim to improve AI-driven candidate evaluation and mitigate bias caused by self-promotion. The dataset is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper possesses moderate novelty. Using LLMs for resume parsing and seniority classification isn't entirely new; existing works have explored similar applications. However, the specific focus on detecting seniority inflation through the creation and use of a *hybrid* dataset *containing synthetically generated hard examples*, designed to explicitly challenge the models' ability to differentiate between genuine experience and inflated claims, adds a valuable layer. The dataset's availability is a commendable contribution promoting reproducibility and further research. Prior work might have focused on overall resume parsing or skill extraction; this paper specifically zeroes in on the difficult task of *accurate seniority assessment* in the face of deliberate ambiguity or exaggeration.

**Significance:** The significance lies in addressing a practical and crucial problem in HR and recruitment. The potential to automate and improve the accuracy of seniority assessment can save time and resources, and reduce potential biases in the hiring process. By training LLMs on a dataset designed to expose weaknesses in recognizing exaggerated qualifications, the research attempts to improve fairness and reduce the influence of superficial self-promotion. This is a problem of real-world importance and has tangible impacts on hiring outcomes. The public availability of the dataset will also enable others to further refine techniques in this area.

**Strengths:**

*   **Hybrid Dataset:** The creation of a hybrid dataset with both real and synthetic resumes, specifically designed with "hard examples" to test for seniority inflation, is a significant strength. This approach allows for more rigorous testing than relying solely on real-world data, which may lack the necessary challenging examples.
*   **Focus on Seniority Inflation:** Addressing the problem of overstated experience is a crucial aspect of accurate resume assessment, and the paper tackles this directly.
*   **Public Dataset:** The public availability of the dataset is valuable for the research community and promotes reproducibility and further advancements in the field.
*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of inaccurate seniority assessment and its consequences.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Model Exploration:** While using fine-tuned BERT models is reasonable, the paper might benefit from exploring a wider range of LLMs, including larger models or models specifically designed for text generation, for the synthetic data creation.
*   **Depth of Analysis:** While the concept of detecting inflation is highlighted, the depth of analysis on the *specific linguistic cues* that the LLMs are picking up on could be improved. A more detailed examination of the model's attention weights or explanation methods would enhance the understanding of its decision-making process.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** While the paper likely includes standard evaluation metrics, a discussion on the limitations of these metrics in the context of seniority assessment (e.g., potential for bias in ground truth labels) would strengthen the analysis. More sophisticated metrics tailored to imbalanced datasets (which are common in seniority classification) would improve the robustness.
*   **Generalizability:** The synthetic data generation process needs to be clearly defined and justified. How closely does it mimic real-world seniority inflation techniques? The generalizability of the results depends on the realism of the synthetic examples.
*   **Comparison to Prior Work:** A more thorough comparison to existing resume parsing and seniority classification techniques is needed. Highlighting the specific advantages and disadvantages of the proposed approach compared to state-of-the-art methods would improve the paper's impact.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper makes a worthwhile contribution to the field of AI-driven recruitment. The creation of a hybrid dataset with hard examples addressing seniority inflation is a valuable innovation. However, the limitations regarding model exploration, depth of linguistic analysis, evaluation metrics, generalizability of synthetic data, and comparison to prior work hold it back from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. While the problem addressed is significant, the paper's execution leaves room for improvement. Therefore, a score of **6** is assigned.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Agentic LLMs for Question Answering over Tabular Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09234v1)
- **Authors**: Rishit Tyagi, Mohit Gupta, Rahul Bouri
- **Abstract**: Question Answering over Tabular Data (Table QA) presents unique challenges due to the diverse structure, size, and data types of real-world tables. The SemEval 2025 Task 8 (DataBench) introduced a benchmark composed of large-scale, domain-diverse datasets to evaluate the ability of models to accurately answer structured queries. We propose a Natural Language to SQL (NL-to-SQL) approach leveraging large language models (LLMs) such as GPT-4o, GPT-4o-mini, and DeepSeek v2:16b to generate SQL queries dynamically. Our system follows a multi-stage pipeline involving example selection, SQL query generation, answer extraction, verification, and iterative refinement. Experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, achieving 70.5\% accuracy on DataBench QA and 71.6\% on DataBench Lite QA, significantly surpassing baseline scores of 26\% and 27\% respectively. This paper details our methodology, experimental results, and alternative approaches, providing insights into the strengths and limitations of LLM-driven Table QA.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an agentic Natural Language to SQL (NL-to-SQL) pipeline leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) for question answering over tabular data (Table QA). It addresses the challenges posed by diverse table structures and query complexities in real-world datasets. The pipeline consists of several stages: example selection, SQL query generation, answer extraction, verification, and iterative refinement. The system was evaluated on the SemEval 2025 Task 8 (DataBench) dataset, demonstrating significant improvements over baseline scores using LLMs like GPT-40, GPT-40-mini, and DeepSeek v2:16b. The paper details the methodology, experimental results, and alternative approaches to highlight the strengths and limitations of LLM-driven Table QA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Pipeline:** The multi-stage pipeline is well-structured and addresses key challenges in NL-to-SQL, such as inaccurate SQL generation, handling complex reasoning, and extracting precise answers.
    *   **Use of LLMs:** The paper effectively leverages the power of LLMs, particularly GPT-40, for dynamic SQL query generation and answer refinement, showing significant gains over traditional approaches.
    *   **Iterative Refinement:** The inclusion of a verification and reprocessing step is a strong point. It directly addresses the issue of hallucinated or incorrect queries, contributing to improved robustness and accuracy.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The paper presents strong empirical evidence, demonstrating a significant performance boost compared to baseline methods on the DataBench dataset.
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The methodology is clearly explained, enabling other researchers to reproduce and build upon their work.
    *   **Addressing Real-World Challenges:** The paper tackles Table QA in the context of real-world datasets, which are known for their complexity and heterogeneity.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Large LLMs:** The reliance on powerful LLMs like GPT-40, while effective, may limit the practical applicability in resource-constrained environments. The system's performance with GPT-40-mini is better, but still requires access to a potentially costly API.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Individual Components:** While the overall pipeline is well-engineered, many of the individual components (e.g., example selection via cosine similarity, CoT prompting) are well-established techniques in NLP. The novelty lies more in the integration and tuning of these components within the specific context of Table QA.
    *   **Error Analysis Could Be More Thorough:** The paper identifies two main error categories (numerical reasoning and categorical misclassification) in the future work section, but lacks detailed error analysis, preventing a deeper understanding of the system's limitations.
    *   **Limited Benchmarking:** While the paper demonstrates superior results on DataBench, benchmarking against other state-of-the-art Table QA systems beyond the baseline would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Reproducibility Limitations:** The dependence on closed-source LLMs (like GPT-40) creates reproducibility challenges for other researchers. While the methodology is detailed, replicating the exact results might not be possible without access to the same models.

*   **Significance:**
