    *   The qualitative analysis (e.g., gate activation analysis) provides insights into the model's behavior.
    * The qualitative results show that the model is actually "thinking" on how to address embodiment limitations

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The explanation of some components could be more detailed.
    *   While the paper covers a wide range of benchmarks, more analysis is needed to understand OmniEVA’s limitations and failure cases in real-world robotic applications, which are very dependant on the robotic arm used and the reliability of it low level controller.
    *   Limited comparison against state-of-the-art object navigation.
    *   The lack of description on the physical robot used.

*   **Impact:** OmniEVA has the potential to influence the design of future embodied AI systems by promoting task-adaptive reasoning and explicit consideration of physical constraints. The new benchmarks will also encourage further research in this direction. However, the practical impact will depend on how well these approaches can generalize to more complex and unstructured real-world environments, which will be very dependant on the reliability of the low level robotic controllers.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of 8.  The paper makes a strong contribution to the field of embodied AI by addressing crucial limitations in existing approaches and proposing a novel framework with demonstrable improvements in performance and planning capabilities. While there is room for further investigation and refinement, OmniEVA represents a significant step forward towards more versatile and practical embodied agents.
It addresses both algorithmic and benchmark aspects. The integration between perception, reasoning and robotics seems to be well executed

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 8/10

### MetaRAG: Metamorphic Testing for Hallucination Detection in RAG Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09360v1)
- **Authors**: Channdeth Sok, David Luz, Yacine Haddam
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed in enterprise applications, yet their reliability remains limited by hallucinations, i.e., confident but factually incorrect information. Existing detection approaches, such as SelfCheckGPT and MetaQA, primarily target standalone LLMs and do not address the unique challenges of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems, where responses must be consistent with retrieved evidence. We therefore present MetaRAG, a metamorphic testing framework for hallucination detection in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. MetaRAG operates in a real-time, unsupervised, black-box setting, requiring neither ground-truth references nor access to model internals, making it suitable for proprietary and high-stakes domains. The framework proceeds in four stages: (1) decompose answers into atomic factoids, (2) generate controlled mutations of each factoid using synonym and antonym substitutions, (3) verify each variant against the retrieved context (synonyms are expected to be entailed and antonyms contradicted), and (4) aggregate penalties for inconsistencies into a response-level hallucination score. Crucially for identity-aware AI, MetaRAG localizes unsupported claims at the factoid span where they occur (e.g., pregnancy-specific precautions, LGBTQ+ refugee rights, or labor eligibility), allowing users to see flagged spans and enabling system designers to configure thresholds and guardrails for identity-sensitive queries. Experiments on a proprietary enterprise dataset illustrate the effectiveness of MetaRAG for detecting hallucinations and enabling trustworthy deployment of RAG-based conversational agents. We also outline a topic-based deployment design that translates MetaRAG's span-level scores into identity-aware safeguards; this design is discussed but not evaluated in our experiments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaRAG, a novel metamorphic testing framework designed for detecting hallucinations in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. Unlike existing methods, MetaRAG operates in a black-box, unsupervised manner, requiring neither ground-truth references nor access to model internals.  It works by decomposing answers into factoids, generating controlled mutations (synonym/antonym substitutions), verifying these variants against the retrieved context, and then aggregating penalties for inconsistencies into a hallucination score. The framework also focuses on identifying unsupported claims at the factoid level, making it possible to implement identity-aware safeguards. The authors evaluate MetaRAG on a proprietary enterprise dataset, demonstrating its effectiveness in detecting hallucinations and improving the trustworthiness of RAG-based conversational agents. They also propose a topic-based deployment design that uses MetaRAG's span-level scores to create identity-aware safeguards.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of metamorphic testing specifically to hallucination detection in RAG systems operating in a black-box environment without ground truth.  While metamorphic testing itself isn't new, its adaptation to this particular problem, especially within the constraints of proprietary datasets and limited access to model internals, is a significant contribution. The factoid decomposition and verification against the retrieved context tailored for RAG scenarios are also novel. Prior MT-based hallucination detection work usually focuses on open-book QA or short outputs. MetaRAG extends the method to a more complex, and realistically constrained industrial setting.

* **Significance:**  The paper addresses a critical challenge in deploying LLMs in enterprise settings: the reliability of RAG systems, especially regarding hallucinations when dealing with proprietary or unseen data.  MetaRAG's black-box nature makes it practical for real-world deployments where access to model internals is often restricted. The emphasis on span-level hallucination detection and integration with identity-aware safeguards is particularly important for mitigating potential harms in sensitive domains (healthcare, law, etc.). The identification of specific problematic factoids offers a considerable practical advantage over simply labeling entire responses as hallucinated. The paper contributes to the growing body of work on making LLMs more trustworthy and reliable, paving the way for their broader adoption in high-stakes applications.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Practical applicability:** MetaRAG's design caters to real-world constraints, requiring no ground truth or internal model access.
    * **Fine-grained detection:**  Span-level hallucination detection allows for more targeted interventions and identity-aware safeguards.
    * **Clear methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the MetaRAG framework and its individual components.
    * **Empirical evaluation:** The evaluation on a proprietary dataset (although it cannot be publicly released) provides evidence of MetaRAG's effectiveness.
    * **Identity-aware deployment design:** The proposal of a topic-based deployment design using MetaRAG scores is a valuable addition.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Proprietary Dataset:** The use of a proprietary dataset limits reproducibility and independent verification of the results.  However, the authors justify this due to confidentiality concerns.
    * **Limited Evaluation of Identity-Aware Safeguards:**  The paper only proposes, but does not empirically evaluate the identity-aware safeguards.  This is a key area for future research.
    * **Model Dependency:** The results are based on GPT-4.1 variants, and its generalizability is not comprehensively established across different LLMs.
    * **Lack of Public Code:** The lack of publicly available code limits the reproducibility.
    * **Lack of Comparison to Strong Baselines:** While the paper describes existing works, a direct comparison to other SOTA black-box hallucination detection methods (e.g. SelfCheckGPT) in the *same* RAG setting on the *same* dataset would strengthen the evaluation.

* **Potential Impact:**  The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of trustworthy AI and RAG systems. MetaRAG's practical design and focus on identity-aware safeguards can contribute to safer and fairer deployments of LLMs in high-stakes applications.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and practical approach to hallucination detection in RAG systems. Its black-box nature, factoid-level detection, and potential integration with identity-aware safeguards make it a valuable contribution. While the use of a proprietary dataset and lack of empirical evaluation of identity-aware safeguards are limitations, the overall significance and potential impact of MetaRAG justify a high score. The strong methodology, clear presentation, and focus on a realistic deployment scenario are further strengths that support the score. A point is deducted for not conducting direct comparisons to other baselines in the same RAG setting with the proprietary dataset, lack of publicly available code, and lack of empirical validation of the proposed identity-aware policies.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Plug-and-play Diffusion Models for Image Compressive Sensing with Data Consistency Projection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09365v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaodong Wang, Ping Wang, Zhangyuan Li, Xin Yuan
- **Abstract**: We explore the connection between Plug-and-Play (PnP) methods and Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIM) for solving ill-posed inverse problems, with a focus on single-pixel imaging. We begin by identifying key distinctions between PnP and diffusion models-particularly in their denoising mechanisms and sampling procedures. By decoupling the diffusion process into three interpretable stages: denoising, data consistency enforcement, and sampling, we provide a unified framework that integrates learned priors with physical forward models in a principled manner. Building upon this insight, we propose a hybrid data-consistency module that linearly combines multiple PnP-style fidelity terms. This hybrid correction is applied directly to the denoised estimate, improving measurement consistency without disrupting the diffusion sampling trajectory. Experimental results on single-pixel imaging tasks demonstrate that our method achieves better reconstruction quality.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper:

The paper explores the relationship between Plug-and-Play (PnP) and Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models (DDIM) for solving ill-posed inverse problems like single-pixel imaging. It highlights differences in denoising and sampling between the two approaches. The authors propose a unified framework that decomposes the diffusion process into denoising, data consistency, and sampling, enabling integration of learned priors with physical forward models. A novel "hybrid data-consistency module," combining PnP-style fidelity terms, is introduced to improve measurement consistency without disrupting the diffusion trajectory. Experiments on single-pixel imaging demonstrate improved reconstruction quality.

Here's a rigorous and critical evaluation of the paper's novelty and significance:

**Novelty:**

*   **Unified Framework:** The decomposition of the DDIM process into denoising, data consistency, and sampling is a useful abstraction that aids in understanding and potentially improving both PnP and diffusion model approaches. This modular view is more than just a re-interpretation; it provides a pathway for tailored modifications to each stage. This is moderately novel.
*   **Hybrid Data-Consistency Module:** The idea of combining multiple PnP-style fidelity terms within the DDIM framework is potentially novel. Combining them *linearly* is a relatively simple approach, but the specific method of combination and its effect on the diffusion trajectory is a non-trivial contribution.
*   **Application to Single-Pixel Imaging:** While applying existing methods to new domains isn't inherently novel, single-pixel imaging is a relevant and challenging application, providing a concrete demonstration of the method's effectiveness in a situation where standard imaging techniques struggle. However, using single-pixel imaging is more of an implementation aspect rather than a core novelty.

**Significance:**

*   **Bridging PnP and Diffusion Models:** The paper contributes to the ongoing effort of understanding and unifying different classes of image reconstruction methods. Bridging PnP and diffusion models is a valuable step in improving image reconstruction performance.
*   **Improved Reconstruction Quality:** The experimental results suggest a tangible improvement in reconstruction quality for single-pixel imaging compared to existing methods (though the extent of the improvement needs to be carefully examined in the paper itself). This practical benefit strengthens the paper's significance.
*   **Potential for Generalization:** While demonstrated on single-pixel imaging, the proposed framework and hybrid data-consistency module have the potential to be generalized to other ill-posed inverse problems in imaging and other domains. This broader applicability increases the potential impact.
*   **Limitations:** The linear combination in the hybrid data-consistency term is a relatively simple choice. The paper does not discuss the limitations of the chosen linear model and potential benefits of using nonlinear combination of different fidelity terms. Also, the computational complexity of the whole process needs to be discussed.

**Critical Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by providing a unifying perspective on PnP and diffusion models and demonstrating a practical improvement in single-pixel imaging reconstruction. The decomposition of the diffusion process and the hybrid data-consistency module offer potential avenues for further research and development. However, the linear combination in the data consistency, although simple, might not be the most optimal approach for combining different regularization terms.

While the improvements reported in single-pixel imaging are meaningful, the generality of the approach and its applicability to other imaging modalities requires further investigation. More rigorous comparison against state-of-the-art PnP and DDIM methods, along with ablations studies focusing on the hybrid data-consistency module are also warranted. The paper doesn't make revolutionary strides but presents a solid incremental advance in the field.

**Score: 6**

*Rationale:* The paper is well-written, technically sound, and presents a novel integration of PnP and diffusion model concepts. The experimental results, particularly on single-pixel imaging, support the claims of improved reconstruction quality. However, the novelty is somewhat limited, and the paper could benefit from a more in-depth exploration of the hybrid data-consistency module and comparison to other state-of-the-art techniques. It offers a practical and conceptually interesting advance, but not a paradigm shift. A score of 6 reflects this balanced assessment.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### MetaLLMix : An XAI Aided LLM-Meta-learning Based Approach for Hyper-parameters Optimization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09387v1)
- **Authors**: Mohammed Tiouti, Mohamed Bal-Ghaoui
- **Abstract**: Effective model and hyperparameter selection remains a major challenge in deep learning, often requiring extensive expertise and computation. While AutoML and large language models (LLMs) promise automation, current LLM-based approaches rely on trial and error and expensive APIs, which provide limited interpretability and generalizability. We propose MetaLLMiX, a zero-shot hyperparameter optimization framework combining meta-learning, explainable AI, and efficient LLM reasoning. By leveraging historical experiment outcomes with SHAP explanations, MetaLLMiX recommends optimal hyperparameters and pretrained models without additional trials. We further employ an LLM-as-judge evaluation to control output format, accuracy, and completeness. Experiments on eight medical imaging datasets using nine open-source lightweight LLMs show that MetaLLMiX achieves competitive or superior performance to traditional HPO methods while drastically reducing computational cost. Our local deployment outperforms prior API-based approaches, achieving optimal results on 5 of 8 tasks, response time reductions of 99.6-99.9%, and the fastest training times on 6 datasets (2.4-15.7x faster), maintaining accuracy within 1-5% of best-performing baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "METALLMIX : AN XAI AIDED LLM-META-LEARNING BASED APPROACH FOR HYPER-PARAMETERS OPTIMIZATION":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MetaLLMiX, a novel framework for zero-shot hyperparameter optimization (HPO) that leverages meta-learning, explainable AI (XAI) using SHAP values, and large language models (LLMs). It aims to automate model selection and hyperparameter tuning without requiring iterative experimentation. The core idea is to use historical experiment outcomes (enriched with SHAP-based explanations) to recommend optimal configurations and models for new datasets.  The framework uses LLMs for reasoning and decision-making, incorporating SHAP values for interpretable explanations. The system has a comprehensive meta-dataset from medical image classification tasks and open-source LLMs. The experiments demonstrate that MetaLLMiX can achieve competitive or superior performance compared to traditional HPO methods, while significantly reducing computational cost, specifically in medical imaging classification tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing concepts (meta-learning, XAI, LLMs for HPO) in a specific and potentially useful way.  The combination of these techniques, the zero-shot approach (avoiding iterative experimentation), and the focus on medical imaging tasks are the main contributions. The use of SHAP values to provide natural language explanations for hyperparameter recommendations is also a good touch. It's an incremental improvement over existing LLM-based HPO solutions, focusing on resource constraints and improved interpretability by integrating SHAP.

*   **Significance:** The claimed benefits are significant: reduced computational cost and increased interpretability for HPO, which could make deep learning more accessible to practitioners, especially in domains where transparency is crucial (like medical imaging). The focus on open-source LLMs makes the approach more accessible and democratized than those depending on expensive proprietary APIs (e.g. GPT-4). The medical image classification tasks are a good choice as the use of AI in healthcare needs to be reliable and explainable.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The zero-shot aspect is a major strength, addressing the computational overhead of many existing LLM-based HPO methods.
    *   The integration of SHAP values improves the transparency of the HPO process.
    *   Using lightweight open-source LLMs makes the system more accessible and less reliant on proprietary APIs.
    *   The extensive experimental evaluation across diverse medical imaging datasets provides solid evidence for the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   The explanation generation quality and LLM outputs evaluation are a plus, assessing the overall approach's utility.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on a meta-dataset presents a potential bottleneck. The quality and diversity of the meta-dataset are critical to the performance of MetaLLMiX. Random sampling to create the meta-dataset isn't optimal and could lead to biased or suboptimal configurations. The selection of datasets in this paper are somewhat limited and doesn't account for real-world datasets.
    *   The generalizability to other domains beyond medical imaging is unclear and needs to be addressed.
    *   While using open-source LLMs is beneficial, the performance variability across different LLMs highlights the challenge of selecting the right LLM for a given task. There isn't any selection metric for the LLM itself that guides the use of the tool in question.
    *   The prompt engineering for the LLM seems highly curated, and the quality and reliability of the output are potentially sensitive to even minor changes to the prompt.
    *   The comparisons, while comprehensive, would benefit from a direct comparison to other AutoML frameworks with transfer learning capabilities, like Auto-sklearn, to more clearly demonstrate the value added by the LLM component.

*   **Impact:** If the claims hold up under broader evaluation, MetaLLMiX could have a significant impact on the adoption of deep learning, particularly in domains where computational resources are limited or where interpretability is paramount.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially valuable approach to zero-shot HPO. The combination of meta-learning, XAI, and LLMs is promising. However, the reliance on a potentially limited meta-dataset and the lack of a clear strategy for LLM selection are major limitations. Also, it is a bit too specific and needs to generalize to a broader problem set. As such, while the contributions are worthwhile and should encourage future research, there is a need to refine the approach, expand the scope, and address the limitations related to the meta-dataset and LLM selection.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### HD-MoE: Hybrid and Dynamic Parallelism for Mixture-of-Expert LLMs with 3D Near-Memory Processing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09420v1)
- **Authors**: Haochen Huang, Shuzhang Zhong, Zhe Zhang, Shuangchen Li, Dimin Niu, Hongzhong Zheng, Runsheng Wang, Meng Li
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) with Mixture-of-Expert (MoE) architectures achieve superior model performance with reduced computation costs, but at the cost of high memory capacity and bandwidth requirements. Near-Memory Processing (NMP) accelerators that stack memory directly on the compute through hybrid bonding have demonstrated high bandwidth with high energy efficiency, becoming a promising architecture for MoE models. However, as NMP accelerators comprise distributed memory and computation, how to map the MoE computation directly determines the LLM inference efficiency. Existing parallel mapping strategies, including Tensor Parallelism (TP) and Expert Parallelism (EP), suffer from either high communication costs or unbalanced computation utilization, leading to inferior efficiency. The dynamic routing mechanism of MoE LLMs further aggravates the efficiency challenges. Therefore, in this paper, we propose HD-MoE to automatically optimize the MoE parallel computation across an NMP accelerator. HD-MoE features an offline automatic hybrid parallel mapping algorithm and an online dynamic scheduling strategy to reduce the communication costs while maximizing the computation utilization. With extensive experimental results, we demonstrate that HD-MoE achieves a speedup ranging from 1.1x to 1.8x over TP, 1.1x to 1.5x over EP, and 1.0x to 1.4x over the baseline Hybrid TP-EP with Compute-Balanced parallelism strategies.
- **Summary**: The paper addresses the challenge of efficiently running Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) on Near-Memory Processing (NMP) accelerators. Current parallelization strategies like Tensor Parallelism (TP) and Expert Parallelism (EP) struggle due to high communication costs or unbalanced computation utilization in the distributed memory architecture of NMP. The paper proposes HD-MoE, a novel approach combining offline hybrid parallel mapping and online dynamic scheduling to reduce communication and maximize computation utilization. Experimental results show HD-MoE outperforms existing methods, demonstrating a speedup ranging from 1.1x to 1.8x over TP, 1.1x to 1.5x over EP, and 1.0x to 1.4x over a hybrid TP-EP approach.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant and important problem: optimizing the execution of MoE models on emerging NMP architectures. This is crucial because MoE models offer superior performance with reduced computation but demand high memory bandwidth, which NMP is designed to address. The existing parallelization strategies (TP, EP) are known to have limitations in this context, making the problem well-motivated.

The proposed HD-MoE approach has several strengths:

*   **Hybrid Parallelism:** Combining the strengths of different parallelization techniques (likely TP and EP) is a logical step towards optimization, potentially mitigating the individual weaknesses of each approach.
*   **Offline Optimization:** The offline automatic mapping algorithm is likely leveraging profiling or analytical modeling to make informed decisions about data and computation placement, which is a good design choice for minimizing communication overhead.
*   **Online Dynamic Scheduling:** Addressing the dynamic routing nature of MoE models is crucial. An online scheduler can adapt to the varying workloads and communication patterns during inference, leading to better performance.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents experimental results comparing HD-MoE against standard methods (TP, EP, Hybrid TP-EP), demonstrating tangible performance improvements. This is vital for validating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

However, there are potential weaknesses and areas that require further scrutiny:

*   **Novelty of the Techniques:** While combining parallelization strategies and using offline/online optimization is promising, the degree of novelty depends on *how* these are implemented. Are the specific algorithms and heuristics truly new, or are they based on existing techniques with minor modifications? The paper needs to elaborate on the technical details of the automatic hybrid parallel mapping algorithm and the online dynamic scheduling strategy.
*   **Scalability Analysis:** The experimental results show speedups, but it's important to understand how these speedups scale with the number of NMP nodes and the size of the MoE model. A comprehensive scalability analysis is necessary to assess the practical applicability of HD-MoE in large-scale deployments.
*   **Complexity Analysis:** The offline mapping algorithm likely has a computational complexity. The paper should discuss the time it takes to perform the offline mapping and how it scales with the model size and NMP architecture. Also, the overhead introduced by the online scheduler must be evaluated, as it could negate some of the performance gains.
*   **Generalizability:** The evaluation likely uses a specific NMP architecture and a specific MoE model. How well does HD-MoE generalize to different NMP platforms and different MoE models (e.g., different numbers of experts, different routing algorithms)?
*   **Implementation Details:** The paper needs to provide sufficient details on the implementation of HD-MoE. This includes the programming language, libraries, and hardware platform used. This allows other researchers to reproduce the results and build upon the work.

Without further details on the specific algorithms used in HD-MoE, it's challenging to assess its true originality. However, the problem being addressed is undoubtedly relevant, and the approach seems promising. The reported speedups, while not groundbreaking, are significant and suggest that HD-MoE offers a valuable improvement over existing methods. Given these considerations, a score of **7** is justified. The paper has the potential to make a significant contribution, but the level of originality and practical impact will depend on the specific details of the algorithms and a more thorough evaluation of scalability and generalizability. The paper provides an initial framework that necessitates further investigation of these critical aspects.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.PF
- **Score**: 7/10

### ENSI: Efficient Non-Interactive Secure Inference for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09424v1)
- **Authors**: Zhiyu He, Maojiang Wang, Xinwen Gao, Yuchuan Luo, Lin Liu, Shaojing Fu
- **Abstract**: Secure inference enables privacy-preserving machine learning by leveraging cryptographic protocols that support computations on sensitive user data without exposing it. However, integrating cryptographic protocols with large language models (LLMs) presents significant challenges, as the inherent complexity of these protocols, together with LLMs' massive parameter scale and sophisticated architectures, severely limits practical usability. In this work, we propose ENSI, a novel non-interactive secure inference framework for LLMs, based on the principle of co-designing the cryptographic protocols and LLM architecture. ENSI employs an optimized encoding strategy that seamlessly integrates CKKS scheme with a lightweight LLM variant, BitNet, significantly reducing the computational complexity of encrypted matrix multiplications. In response to the prohibitive computational demands of softmax under homomorphic encryption (HE), we pioneer the integration of the sigmoid attention mechanism with HE as a seamless, retraining-free alternative. Furthermore, by embedding the Bootstrapping operation within the RMSNorm process, we efficiently refresh ciphertexts while markedly decreasing the frequency of costly bootstrapping invocations. Experimental evaluations demonstrate that ENSI achieves approximately an 8x acceleration in matrix multiplications and a 2.6x speedup in softmax inference on CPU compared to state-of-the-art method, with the proportion of bootstrapping is reduced to just 1%.
- **Summary**: ## Summary:

This paper introduces ENSI, a new non-interactive secure inference framework for Large Language Models (LLMs). ENSI addresses the challenges of integrating cryptographic protocols with LLMs by co-designing the cryptography and LLM architecture. It achieves this through three key innovations: 1) an optimized encoding strategy integrating the CKKS scheme with the BitNet LLM variant to reduce encrypted matrix multiplication complexity; 2) replacing softmax with the sigmoid attention mechanism (without retraining) for efficient homomorphic encryption (HE) compatibility; and 3) embedding the computationally expensive Bootstrapping operation within the RMSNorm process to reduce its frequency. Experiments show ENSI achieves significant speedups in matrix multiplications and softmax inference compared to existing methods, with drastically reduced bootstrapping overhead.

## Critical Evaluation:

The paper presents a compelling approach to secure inference for LLMs, tackling the significant challenges posed by their size and complexity. The strength of the work lies in its holistic, co-design approach. Instead of simply trying to apply existing HE techniques to existing LLMs, ENSI rethinks both the cryptographic protocols and the LLM architecture to achieve practical performance.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty in Co-design:** The co-design strategy, where cryptographic protocols and LLM architecture are considered together, is a key strength. This allows for targeted optimizations that wouldn't be possible otherwise.
*   **Practical Performance Gains:** The reported speedups in matrix multiplication and softmax inference are significant. These are critical bottlenecks in secure inference and addressing them demonstrably advances the field. The reduction in bootstrapping frequency is particularly important as it's one of the most expensive HE operations.
*   **Retraining-Free Softmax Replacement:** The integration of the sigmoid attention mechanism without requiring retraining is a significant advantage. This makes the approach more readily applicable to existing LLMs without the need for costly and potentially data-leaky retraining.
*   **Well-defined contributions:** Each of the three innovations (encoding strategy, sigmoid attention, and bootstrapping within RMSNorm) is clearly defined and contributes to the overall performance improvement.
*   **Use of BitNet:** Utilizing BitNet, a more lightweight LLM architecture, is a sensible choice to reduce the computational burden of secure inference, and shows a good understanding of the current landscape of LLM research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Scope of LLM Architecture:** While the choice of BitNet is justified, it also limits the generalizability of the results. The performance improvements might not directly translate to more complex and widely used architectures like Transformers. This needs to be explicitly acknowledged and ideally explored, even if partially. More generalizable results would significantly increase the impact of the work.
*   **Lack of Security Analysis:** The paper focuses primarily on performance improvements. A more thorough security analysis, going beyond just mentioning the use of CKKS, is needed. For example, what are the limitations of the CKKS scheme in this particular application? How resistant is the system to chosen ciphertext attacks, especially given the modifications made to the standard CKKS operations? What are the noise growth characteristics within their custom design. These are important questions that need to be addressed to assess the true security level of ENSI.
*   **Practical Deployment Considerations:** While the paper demonstrates improvements in secure inference speed, it doesn't fully address the practical aspects of deploying such a system. For example, how much memory is required for the encrypted model and data? How does the latency scale with the size of the input sequence? These practical considerations are crucial for real-world adoption.
*   **Comparison to other HE schemes:** There needs to be some comparison to other potential HE schemes. While CKKS is a common one, is it truly the most suitable for this type of application? Are there other schemes which might have better performance trade-offs for this task?
*   **Overstated Bootstrapping Reduction:** While a reduction in bootstrapping to 1% is impressive, the impact should be stated carefully. If that 1% is still a significant computational bottleneck, the overall benefit might be less substantial than implied. The breakdown of computation time for each component is critical.

**Significance:**

The paper is significant because it moves towards making secure inference of LLMs more practical. The co-design approach and specific optimizations address key bottlenecks in existing methods. The retraining-free replacement of softmax with sigmoid attention is a particularly valuable contribution. However, the limited scope of the LLM architecture and the lack of a thorough security analysis limit its overall impact.

**Score:** 7

**Justification:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's strengths in its novel co-design approach, practical performance gains, and retraining-free sigmoid attention mechanism. The demonstrated speedups are significant and contribute to advancing the field. However, the weaknesses in the limited scope of the LLM architecture (restricting the broad applicability of the findings), the lack of in-depth security analysis, and the lack of practical deployment considerations prevent a higher score. The potential impact is considerable, but more rigorous validation across different LLM architectures and a comprehensive security analysis are needed to fully realize its promise. A deeper investigation and comparison with other HE schemes is warranted. Finally, more precise data regarding the proportion of computational load during Bootstrapping is needed.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### GrACE: A Generative Approach to Better Confidence Elicitation in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09438v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaohan Zhang, Ziquan Liu, Ioannis Patras
- **Abstract**: Assessing the reliability of Large Language Models (LLMs) by confidence elicitation is a prominent approach to AI safety in high-stakes applications, such as healthcare and finance. Existing methods either require expensive computational overhead or suffer from poor calibration, making them impractical and unreliable for real-world deployment. In this work, we propose GrACE, a Generative Approach to Confidence Elicitation that enables scalable and reliable confidence elicitation for LLMs. GrACE adopts a novel mechanism in which the model expresses confidence by the similarity between the last hidden state and the embedding of a special token appended to the vocabulary, in real-time. We fine-tune the model for calibrating the confidence with calibration targets associated with accuracy. Experiments with three LLMs and two benchmark datasets show that the confidence produced by GrACE achieves the best discriminative capacity and calibration on open-ended generation tasks, outperforming six competing methods without resorting to additional sampling or an auxiliary model. Moreover, we propose two strategies for improving test-time scaling based on confidence induced by GrACE. Experimental results show that using GrACE not only improves the accuracy of the final decision but also significantly reduces the number of required samples in the test-time scaling scheme, indicating the potential of GrACE as a practical solution for deploying LLMs with scalable, reliable, and real-time confidence estimation.
- **Summary**: ## Concise Summary:

This paper introduces GrACE, a novel and efficient method for calibrating confidence in Large Language Models (LLMs) during open-ended generation. GrACE fine-tunes the LLM to express confidence by measuring the similarity between its last hidden state and the embedding of a special token added to the vocabulary. The confidence score is calibrated against accuracy targets during training. Experiments demonstrate GrACE's superior performance in both discriminative capacity and calibration compared to existing methods, without incurring significant computational overhead. Furthermore, the paper proposes and validates strategies for improving test-time scaling using GrACE's confidence scores, leading to improved accuracy and reduced sampling requirements.

## Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:

**Strengths:**

* **Novelty:** The approach of using a special token and hidden state similarity for confidence estimation is relatively novel. While other methods exist for confidence calibration, GrACE's method is distinct in its simplicity and efficiency, integrating confidence estimation directly into the LLM's forward pass.
* **Efficiency:** A key selling point is GrACE's low computational overhead. Existing methods often rely on multiple forward passes or auxiliary models, which can be prohibitive for large LLMs. GrACE avoids these costs, making it a more practical solution for real-world deployment.
* **Performance:** The paper presents convincing experimental results demonstrating GrACE's superior performance in calibration and discriminative capacity compared to several baselines across different LLMs and datasets.
* **Test-Time Scaling Application:** The paper goes beyond simply proposing a confidence estimation method by demonstrating its practical utility in improving test-time scaling. The reduction in required samples is a valuable contribution.
* **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the GrACE method and its benefits.

**Weaknesses:**

* **Limited Scope of Datasets and Models:** While the experiments are compelling, they are conducted on a relatively limited number of datasets and LLMs. Further validation across a wider range of tasks and model architectures would strengthen the claims of generalizability. The chosen datasets are not extremely challenging, which might not fully expose potential limitations in edge cases.
* **Special Token Dependency:** The reliance on a special token might introduce some sensitivity to the choice of token and its initial embedding. The paper doesn't thoroughly investigate this sensitivity or provide guidelines for optimal token selection. The performance might be significantly affected by different choices.
* **Fine-tuning Requirement:** While more efficient than methods using auxiliary models, GrACE still requires fine-tuning. This raises questions about the adaptation cost when deploying GrACE to new tasks or models, and whether catastrophic forgetting will occur. The paper doesn't adequately address how GrACE performs under continual learning settings.
* **Limited Comparison to State-of-the-Art:** While the paper compares against several existing methods, the performance of GrACE compared to recent advances in uncertainty quantification and confidence calibration techniques might not be fully explored. Specifically, it's important to see its performance compared to methods tailored to improve out-of-distribution detection or adversarial robustness.
* **Overclaiming:** The title suggests the method achieves "scalable, reliable, and real-time confidence estimation." While the claims of efficiency (scaling and real-time) are well-supported, the reliability claim might be overstated given the limited scope of evaluation. The performance on complex or safety-critical tasks could reveal limitations not captured in the current experiments.

**Significance:**

GrACE represents a significant step towards more practical and reliable confidence elicitation for LLMs. Its efficiency and strong performance make it a promising candidate for real-world deployment in high-stakes applications. The integration of confidence estimation directly into the LLM's architecture, rather than relying on external methods, is a valuable design choice. The application to test-time scaling further enhances the practical relevance of the work. The paper can motivate further research into efficient and effective methods for quantifying uncertainty in LLMs and could lead to its adoption in some commercial deployments.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering both the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7**.

* **It surpasses mediocrity**: The paper presents a novel and efficient solution to a significant problem.
* **It has potential for good impact:** The improvement in confidence estimation and its application to test-time scaling are valuable contributions.
* **It has some limitations:** The limited scope of evaluation and the reliance on fine-tuning prevent it from achieving a higher score. The reliance on a single special token and its potential sensitivity is also a notable drawback.
* **It can be improved:** Further research is needed to address the weaknesses and validate the robustness of GrACE in more challenging scenarios. More rigorous comparison to state-of-the-art methods would strengthen the paper's claims.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TORSO: Template-Oriented Reasoning Towards General Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09448v1)
- **Authors**: Minhyuk Kim, Seungyoon Lee, Heuiseok Lim
- **Abstract**: The approaches that guide Large Language Models (LLMs) to emulate human reasoning during response generation have emerged as an effective method for enabling them to solve complex problems in a step-by-step manner, thereby achieving superior performance. However, most existing approaches using few-shot prompts to generate responses heavily depend on the provided examples, limiting the utilization of the model's inherent reasoning capabilities. Moreover, constructing task-specific few-shot prompts is often costly and may lead to inconsistencies across different tasks. In this work, we introduce Template-Oriented Reasoning (TORSO), which elicits the model to utilize internal reasoning abilities to generate proper responses across various tasks without the need for manually crafted few-shot examples. Our experimental results demonstrate that TORSO achieves strong performance on diverse LLMs benchmarks with reasonable rationales.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Template-Oriented Reasoning (TORSO), a method designed to guide Large Language Models (LLMs) to leverage their inherent reasoning capabilities without relying on task-specific few-shot prompts or additional training data.  TORSO manipulates the LLM's decoding process by injecting specific tokens at the beginning (to activate reasoning) and end (to generate a direct answer) of the response.  Experimental results on various LLM benchmarks demonstrate that TORSO outperforms baseline methods that use in-context learning and achieves strong performance with reasonable rationales. The paper also includes qualitative analysis of the generated rationales and ablation studies to assess the impact of the chosen template.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of TORSO – manipulating the logit probabilities during decoding to guide LLMs toward reasoning without few-shot examples or training data – is fairly novel. While prompting and logit biasing techniques exist, the specific combination of triggering reasoning with initial tokens and encouraging direct answer generation at the end is a valuable contribution. The idea of *unlocking* inherent reasoning abilities is interesting and addresses a key challenge with standard prompting which can "overfit" to demonstration examples.

*   **Significance:**  The potential significance lies in reducing the need for extensive prompt engineering and curated training data for each new task. Few-shot learning can be brittle, and creating high-quality reasoning datasets is costly. If TORSO can consistently unlock reasoning in a generalizable way, it could simplify the deployment of LLMs across various domains. This has practical implications for real-world applications, as it makes LLMs more accessible to users who lack the expertise or resources to create specialized prompts.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The experimental results show a consistent improvement over strong baselines, including zero-shot CoT and other in-context learning techniques.
    *   The qualitative analysis provides evidence that the rationales generated by TORSO are often more appropriate than those of the baselines.
    *   The ablation study offers valuable insights into the importance of the chosen template and the impact of injecting meaningless tokens.
    *   The length efficiency analysis is a strong point, as it shows that TORSO can achieve strong performance with shorter generations and without relying on lengthy demonstrations.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper acknowledges that TORSO's effectiveness may be limited for tasks requiring reasoning over newly emerging information or those outside the model's training distribution. This is a significant limitation, as it restricts the applicability of the method.
    *   The evaluation relies primarily on exact match accuracy, which may not fully capture the nuances of reasoning quality. Metrics that assess the correctness and completeness of the generated rationales would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the types of reasoning that TORSO is most effective at eliciting. Are there specific tasks or reasoning styles for which TORSO performs particularly well or poorly?
    *   Generalization could be improved by experimentation with a wider array of models, especially the larger models which might be able to utilize the approach even more effectively.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   TORSO can inspire future research on methods for manipulating the decoding process of LLMs to guide their behavior without relying on explicit demonstrations or training data.
    *   The idea of "unlocking" inherent reasoning abilities could lead to new techniques for improving the generalizability and robustness of LLMs.
    *   The method could be integrated with other reasoning techniques, such as chain-of-thought prompting or tree-of-thought, to further enhance their performance.
*   **Rigorous Rationale:** TORSO, despite its simplicity, introduces a valuable way to elicit reasoning from models without few-shot examples, making it practical for real-world use. The quantitative gains are consistent in a wide variety of datasets, showing robustness. Furthermore, the qualitative analysis validates the quality of the reasoning elicited. The method's limitation on tasks outside the models original training distribution is a valid weakness, but can be circumvented by utilizing retrieval augmentation techniques.

**Score: 7**

**Justification:** TORSO presents a novel approach to eliciting reasoning from LLMs by manipulating the decoding process. The method shows strong experimental results, is efficient in terms of length, and offers insights into the importance of the chosen template. While the method is limited to reasoning within the model's training distribution, its simplicity and effectiveness make it a valuable contribution to the field and may inspire future research directions. It therefore deserves a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion Model for Multi-Conditional Molecular Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09451v1)
- **Authors**: Anjie Qiao, Zhen Wang, Chuan Chen, DeFu Lian, Enhong Chen
- **Abstract**: Controllable molecular graph generation is essential for material and drug discovery, where generated molecules must satisfy diverse property constraints. While recent advances in graph diffusion models have improved generation quality, their effectiveness in multi-conditional settings remains limited due to reliance on joint conditioning or continuous relaxations that compromise fidelity. To address these limitations, we propose Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion model (CSGD), the first model that extends score matching to discrete graphs via concrete scores, enabling flexible and principled manipulation of conditional guidance. Building on this foundation, we introduce two score-based techniques: Composable Guidance (CoG), which allows fine-grained control over arbitrary subsets of conditions during sampling, and Probability Calibration (PC), which adjusts estimated transition probabilities to mitigate train-test mismatches. Empirical results on four molecular datasets show that CSGD achieves state-of-the-art performance, with a 15.3% average improvement in controllability over prior methods, while maintaining high validity and distributional fidelity. Our findings highlight the practical advantages of score-based modeling for discrete graph generation and its capacity for flexible, multi-property molecular design.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper:

The paper introduces Composable Score-based Graph Diffusion (CSGD), a novel graph diffusion model for controllable molecular graph generation. CSGD utilizes concrete scores to extend score matching to discrete graphs, allowing for flexible and principled manipulation of conditional guidance. Two key techniques, Composable Guidance (CoG) and Probability Calibration (PC), are introduced to enable fine-grained control over conditions and mitigate train-test discrepancies, respectively. Experimental results demonstrate that CSGD outperforms existing methods in multi-conditional molecular generation, exhibiting significant improvements in controllability while maintaining high validity and fidelity.

Now, a rigorous and critical evaluation:

**Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects.

*   **Concrete Scores for Discrete Graph Diffusion:** Extending score matching to discrete graphs via concrete scores is a key contribution. While score-based methods are established in continuous spaces, adapting them to the discrete nature of graphs, particularly molecules, is non-trivial. This tackles a limitation of existing methods that often rely on continuous relaxations, which can lead to inaccuracies in the generated graphs.

*   **Composable Guidance (CoG):** The ability to control arbitrary subsets of conditions during sampling is a significant advancement. Existing multi-conditional generation methods often struggle with disentangling the effects of different conditions or fail to provide fine-grained control. CoG allows users to prioritize or selectively apply constraints during generation, which can be crucial in practical applications.

*   **Probability Calibration (PC):** Addressing the train-test mismatch in diffusion models is a crucial step towards improving the reliability of generated samples. PC specifically aims to improve the accuracy of transition probabilities, thereby reducing the likelihood of generating invalid or unrealistic structures.

**Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in molecular design: controllable generation of molecules satisfying multiple, often conflicting, property constraints. Successfully addressing this challenge has significant implications for drug discovery and materials science, where the ability to generate molecules with desired properties is highly valuable. The reported improvements in controllability are substantial (15.3% average improvement), suggesting that CSGD offers a practical advantage over existing methods. The emphasis on validity and distributional fidelity is also important, ensuring that the generated molecules are both chemically plausible and diverse.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing graph diffusion models in multi-conditional settings.
*   **Technically Sound Methodology:** The proposed techniques (concrete scores, CoG, and PC) are well-motivated and technically sound. The use of concrete scores provides a principled way to perform score matching in discrete spaces.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The experimental evaluation is thorough and demonstrates the effectiveness of CSGD across multiple molecular datasets. The reported improvements in controllability are significant.
*   **Well-Written and Organized:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand. The methodology is clearly explained, and the experimental results are presented in a convincing manner.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Complexity:** While the paper demonstrates improved performance, it would benefit from a discussion of the computational cost of CSGD compared to existing methods. Score-based methods can be computationally expensive, and the overhead of using concrete scores and CoG/PC needs to be considered. The runtime performance is not thoroughly addressed.
*   **Scalability:** While the paper evaluates on molecular datasets, it remains to be seen how well CSGD scales to larger and more complex graph structures. Graph neural networks often struggle with very large graphs, and the performance of CSGD in such settings needs to be assessed.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper provides a good explanation of the practical benefits of CoG and PC but lacks a more in-depth theoretical analysis of their properties and limitations. Understanding the theoretical guarantees of these techniques would further strengthen the paper.
*   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The paper should discuss the sensitivity of the performance of CSGD to different hyperparameters, particularly those related to the diffusion process and the concrete scores.

**Potential Influence:** CSGD has the potential to significantly influence the field of molecular graph generation. The introduction of concrete scores and the development of CoG and PC provide valuable tools for controllable molecular design. The paper's findings are likely to inspire further research in this area, focusing on improving the efficiency and scalability of score-based graph diffusion models. The code availability (if released) will significantly accelerate adoption and further research.

**Justification for Score:** Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **8**. The paper presents a significant advancement in controllable molecular graph generation by introducing a novel score-based approach that addresses the limitations of existing methods. The experimental results are compelling and demonstrate the practical benefits of CSGD. However, the paper could be strengthened by addressing the computational complexity, scalability, and theoretical limitations mentioned above. While impactful, these areas need further work.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### FlexiD-Fuse: Flexible number of inputs multi-modal medical image fusion based on diffusion model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09456v1)
- **Authors**: Yushen Xu, Xiaosong Li, Yuchun Wang, Xiaoqi Cheng, Huafeng Li, Haishu Tan
- **Abstract**: Different modalities of medical images provide unique physiological and anatomical information for diseases. Multi-modal medical image fusion integrates useful information from different complementary medical images with different modalities, producing a fused image that comprehensively and objectively reflects lesion characteristics to assist doctors in clinical diagnosis. However, existing fusion methods can only handle a fixed number of modality inputs, such as accepting only two-modal or tri-modal inputs, and cannot directly process varying input quantities, which hinders their application in clinical settings. To tackle this issue, we introduce FlexiD-Fuse, a diffusion-based image fusion network designed to accommodate flexible quantities of input modalities. It can end-to-end process two-modal and tri-modal medical image fusion under the same weight. FlexiD-Fuse transforms the diffusion fusion problem, which supports only fixed-condition inputs, into a maximum likelihood estimation problem based on the diffusion process and hierarchical Bayesian modeling. By incorporating the Expectation-Maximization algorithm into the diffusion sampling iteration process, FlexiD-Fuse can generate high-quality fused images with cross-modal information from source images, independently of the number of input images. We compared the latest two and tri-modal medical image fusion methods, tested them on Harvard datasets, and evaluated them using nine popular metrics. The experimental results show that our method achieves the best performance in medical image fusion with varying inputs. Meanwhile, we conducted extensive extension experiments on infrared-visible, multi-exposure, and multi-focus image fusion tasks with arbitrary numbers, and compared them with the perspective SOTA methods. The results of the extension experiments consistently demonstrate the effectiveness and superiority of our method.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FlexiD-Fuse, a novel diffusion-based medical image fusion network that can handle a flexible number of input modalities (e.g., two or three). It addresses the limitation of existing methods that are designed for a fixed number of modalities. FlexiD-Fuse formulates the fusion as a maximum likelihood estimation problem based on the diffusion process and hierarchical Bayesian modeling, using the Expectation-Maximization algorithm within the diffusion sampling. Experimental results on medical datasets and other image fusion tasks (infrared-visible, multi-exposure, multi-focus) demonstrate its superior performance compared to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The primary novelty lies in the network's ability to handle a variable number of input modalities for medical image fusion in an end-to-end manner.  Existing methods typically require separate training or adaptation for different modality combinations.  The adaptation of diffusion models and hierarchical Bayesian modeling to solve this problem is also innovative. The incorporation of the Expectation-Maximization algorithm within the diffusion process further contributes to the novelty.

**Significance:**

The significance stems from the practical applicability of a flexible modality fusion method in clinical settings.  In real-world scenarios, the availability of specific imaging modalities can vary. A system that can adapt to this variability without requiring retraining or extensive modifications is highly valuable. The demonstration of the method's efficacy on diverse fusion tasks, including medical and non-medical, strengthens its potential impact.  The improved fusion performance demonstrated by the experimental results also adds to the significance. If the improvements in fusion translate to real medical benefit is to be verified by medical experts on realistic patient scans.

**Strengths:**

*   **Flexibility:** The ability to handle varying input modalities is a significant advancement.
*   **Diffusion Model Adaptation:** The innovative use of diffusion models combined with Bayesian modeling for fusion is a strong point.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents results on multiple datasets (Harvard medical datasets, infrared-visible, multi-exposure, and multi-focus) and uses several evaluation metrics, lending credibility to the claims.
*   **Comparison to SOTA:** The comparison with state-of-the-art methods in different fusion tasks effectively demonstrates the superiority of the proposed approach.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are known for their computational intensity. While the paper claims to achieve high-quality fusion, the computational cost compared to other (perhaps less flexible) methods needs to be addressed. This is only briefly mentioned. A thorough timing comparison would be necessary.
*   **Generality Assumptions:** The assumption underlying the Bayesian modeling and EM algorithm needs to be verified. In order to achieve good results, the underlying image generative process should satisfy a certain amount of consistency.
*   **Black Box Nature:** Diffusion models, especially when combined with other complex techniques like EM, can be difficult to interpret. Understanding *why* the model produces certain outputs is challenging, hindering potential debugging and improvements.
*   **Limited Clinical Validation:** The paper demonstrates improved fusion quality using standard metrics. However, it lacks direct validation of the clinical utility of the fused images. It's crucial to assess whether the fused images actually lead to better diagnostic accuracy or improved treatment planning by medical experts.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and potentially impactful approach to medical image fusion, the limitations regarding computational cost, the generalizability assumptions, the interpretability of the model, and, most importantly, the lack of direct clinical validation prevent it from receiving a higher score. The flexibility is a major selling point, but practical considerations like computational efficiency and clinical relevance are crucial for real-world adoption.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Changing the Paradigm from Dynamic Queries to LLM-generated SQL Queries with Human Intervention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09461v1)
- **Authors**: Ambre Assor, Hyeon Jeon, Sungbok Shin, Jean-Daniel Fekete
- **Abstract**: We propose leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) as an interaction layer for medical visualization systems. In domains like healthcare, where users must navigate high-dimensional, coded, and heterogeneous datasets, LLM-generated queries enable expert medical users to express complex analytical intents in natural language. These intents are then translated into editable and executable queries, replacing the dynamic query interfaces used by traditional visualization systems built around sliders, check boxes, and drop-downs. This interaction model reduces visual clutter and eliminates the need for users to memorize field names or system codes, supporting fluid exploration, with the drawback of not exposing all the filtering criteria. We also reintroduce dynamic queries on demand to better support interactive exploration. We posit that medical users are trained to know the possible filtering options but challenged to remember the details of the attribute names and code values. We demonstrate this paradigm in ParcoursVis, our scalable EventFlow-inspired patient care pathway visualization system powered by the French National Health Data System, one of the largest health data repositories in the world.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary of the paper:

The paper proposes using Large Language Models (LLMs) as a natural language interface for medical visualization systems. This allows medical experts to formulate complex queries in natural language, which the LLM translates into executable queries for the visualization system. This aims to simplify interaction, reduce visual clutter, and eliminate the need for users to remember specific codes or field names. The system, ParcoursVis, is demonstrated using the French National Health Data System. The authors acknowledge the drawback of not exposing all filtering options upfront but argue that medical experts are already familiar with available options, just not necessarily their technical details. Dynamic queries are also re-introduced on demand for more interactive exploration.

Now, for the rigorous and critical evaluation:

**Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging LLMs for query construction in visualization systems isn't entirely novel in itself. Many researchers have explored natural language interfaces for data exploration and visualization. However, the specific application to *medical* data visualization, particularly with a focus on patient care pathways and using a large, real-world dataset like the French National Health Data System, adds a degree of novelty. The combination of LLM-driven queries with the *re-introduction* of dynamic filtering for interactive exploration is a valuable contribution and moves beyond a simple replacement of existing interfaces. Furthermore, the explicit acknowledgement and addressing of the trade-offs associated with this approach (namely, the limited visibility of all filtering options) is a positive sign.

**Significance:** The significance lies in potentially making complex medical data more accessible and understandable to clinicians and researchers. If successful, this approach could improve decision-making, uncover patterns in patient care pathways, and ultimately lead to better patient outcomes. The use of a large, real-world dataset increases the impact of the research. However, the paper's significance is contingent on demonstrating the *effectiveness* of the LLM-driven interface in improving user performance and insights compared to traditional methods. The paper's success also relies heavily on the performance of the LLM, including its ability to accurately translate natural language queries and understand the context of medical data. The reintroduction of dynamic filtering is important, as the lack of visual affordances has been shown in previous research to lead to mistakes, biased explorations, and lack of transparency. Therefore, evaluating the ease and efficiency of this "on demand" introduction is critical. The paper needs to provide robust user studies or case studies to quantify the benefits and limitations. Without such empirical evidence, the significance remains theoretical. Furthermore, the authors should address the potential ethical concerns surrounding the use of LLMs in healthcare, particularly regarding data privacy, bias, and the potential for misinterpretation of LLM-generated insights.

**Strengths:**

*   Focus on a real-world problem with high potential impact.
*   Uses a large, national-scale dataset.
*   Combines LLM-driven queries with dynamic filtering.
*   Acknowledges limitations and trade-offs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   Lack of concrete evaluation metrics and results. User studies or case studies are crucial for demonstrating the practical value.
*   Potential dependence on the specific LLM used. The generalizability of the approach needs to be addressed.
*   Limited discussion of ethical considerations.
*   Unclear whether the system accounts for the medical data's inherent biases. The LLM needs to be aware of and mitigate such biases.
*   The "on demand" reintroduction of dynamic queries needs to be evaluated for ease of use and efficiency. How many steps are required to reveal all the available filtering options, or specific filtering options based on previous queries?

**Justification for Score:**

While the idea has merit and the application to a real-world medical context is promising, the lack of substantial evaluation significantly weakens the paper. The novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The potential impact is high, but contingent on the effectiveness and usability of the proposed system. Without rigorous evaluation and a thorough discussion of ethical considerations, the paper primarily presents an *idea* rather than a validated solution. For these reasons, I assign the following score:

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 6/10

### Database Views as Explanations for Relational Deep Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09482v1)
- **Authors**: Agapi Rissaki, Ilias Fountalis, Wolfgang Gatterbauer, Benny Kimelfeld
- **Abstract**: In recent years, there has been significant progress in the development of deep learning models over relational databases, including architectures based on heterogeneous graph neural networks (hetero-GNNs) and heterogeneous graph transformers. In effect, such architectures state how the database records and links (e.g., foreign-key references) translate into a large, complex numerical expression, involving numerous learnable parameters. This complexity makes it hard to explain, in human-understandable terms, how a model uses the available data to arrive at a given prediction. We present a novel framework for explaining machine-learning models over relational databases, where explanations are view definitions that highlight focused parts of the database that mostly contribute to the model's prediction. We establish such global abductive explanations by adapting the classic notion of determinacy by Nash, Segoufin, and Vianu (2010). In addition to tuning the tradeoff between determinacy and conciseness, the framework allows controlling the level of granularity by adopting different fragments of view definitions, such as ones highlighting whole columns, foreign keys between tables, relevant groups of tuples, and so on. We investigate the realization of the framework in the case of hetero-GNNs. We develop heuristic algorithms that avoid the exhaustive search over the space of all databases. We propose techniques that are model-agnostic, and others that are tailored to hetero-GNNs via the notion of learnable masking. Our approach is evaluated through an extensive empirical study on the RelBench collection, covering a variety of domains and different record-level tasks. The results demonstrate the usefulness of the proposed explanations, as well as the efficiency of their generation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the paper and here's a summary, evaluation, and score:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel framework for explaining deep relational learning (RDL) models, particularly those based on heterogeneous Graph Neural Networks (hetero-GNNs). The approach uses database views as explanations, where a view definition highlights a focused part of the database that contributes most to the model's prediction. This "soft determinacy" approach adapts the classical notion of determinacy to account for the inherent complexities and approximate nature of machine learning models. The framework supports various levels of granularity and allows users to control the trade-off between explanation accuracy and conciseness.  The paper develops heuristic algorithms tailored to hetero-GNNs, implements the framework on the RelBench collection of databases, and presents an empirical evaluation demonstrating the utility and efficiency of the generated explanations.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a fresh perspective on explaining RDL models by grounding explanations in the familiar language of database views (SQL). While explanation methods exist for GNNs, this work specifically targets RDL and proposes a mechanism that directly relates the explanation to the original data source (relational database). Adapting determinacy to a soft, statistical setting and applying it to the problem of explaining complex relational machine learning models is a novel idea. The use of mask learning to optimize these explanations is also a noteworthy technical contribution. The novelty comes from combining ideas from database theory (determinacy), machine learning explainability (masking), and the burgeoning field of RDL.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper lies in its ability to bridge the gap between complex RDL models and human understanding. RDL models, by their very nature, operate on structured data and are often used in domains where interpretability is crucial. This framework makes these models more accessible and trustworthy by providing explanations that can be readily understood by database users and domain experts. Providing explanations in the form of SQL views is a practical advantage, as database professionals can readily interpret and validate them. By improving model understanding, users can better detect biases, improve model performance, and make more informed decisions. Showing that models can be trained with similar quality with just a fraction of the data provides further justification of its usefulness.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-defined framework based on theoretical foundations (determinacy).
    *   Practical approach using SQL view definitions for explanations.
    *   Efficient heuristic algorithms tailored for hetero-GNNs.
    *   Comprehensive empirical evaluation on a standard RDL benchmark (RelBench).
    *   Address an important need for interpretability in RDL models.
    *   Shows that model can be trained with similar quality with just a fraction of the data

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The heuristic algorithms are specifically designed for hetero-GNNs. The paper lacks discussion of how the soft-determinacy principles can be applied in other settings.
    *   While the empirical evaluation is thorough, it primarily focuses on node-level tasks. The effectiveness of the framework on other RDL tasks, such as link prediction or graph classification, remains to be explored.
    *   The cost model for explanation conciseness is relatively simple. More sophisticated cost models could potentially lead to more informative and user-friendly explanations.
    *   The framework assumes instance-agnostic views. While there's value in exploring instance-specific views for more granular and targeted explanations, instance-specific explanations may lead to complications in terms of model complexity, search space, and computation overhead.
    *   The work does not compare its methods to other global graph-based explanation approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of RDL by providing a much-needed explanation framework. It can serve as a foundation for further research on explainable RDL, inspiring the development of more sophisticated explanation techniques and algorithms. The framework could be adopted by practitioners who seek to improve the interpretability and trustworthiness of their RDL models. The grounding of explanations in SQL could foster collaboration between machine learning and database communities.

**Score:** 7.5/10

**Justification:**

The paper represents a significant contribution to the field of RDL by introducing a novel and practical explanation framework. It bridges the gap between complex models and human understanding, making RDL more accessible and trustworthy. The theoretical grounding in determinacy, the efficient heuristic algorithms, and the comprehensive empirical evaluation are all strengths of the paper. The weaknesses, such as the limited scope of the heuristic algorithms and the simple cost model, are areas for future research but do not detract significantly from the overall contribution. The paper has the potential to influence the field and inspire further research on explainable RDL.

I would have rated it higher (8+) if it presented a direct comparison to at least one baseline graph explanation model. That said, given that it is designed to be database-centric and operates by manipulating the database itself, perhaps no baseline graph explanation method provides a fair comparison. Also, the fact that SQL view creation and use of determinacy are novel ideas is a substantial benefit.

**Note:** I've tried to be rigorous and critical in my assessment, focusing on both the strengths and weaknesses of the paper and providing a well-reasoned justification for the score.

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Prompt Pirates Need a Map: Stealing Seeds helps Stealing Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.09488v1)
- **Authors**: Felix Mächtle, Ashwath Shetty, Jonas Sander, Nils Loose, Sören Pirk, Thomas Eisenbarth
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have significantly advanced text-to-image generation, enabling the creation of highly realistic images conditioned on textual prompts and seeds. Given the considerable intellectual and economic value embedded in such prompts, prompt theft poses a critical security and privacy concern. In this paper, we investigate prompt-stealing attacks targeting diffusion models. We reveal that numerical optimization-based prompt recovery methods are fundamentally limited as they do not account for the initial random noise used during image generation. We identify and exploit a noise-generation vulnerability (CWE-339), prevalent in major image-generation frameworks, originating from PyTorch's restriction of seed values to a range of $2^{32}$ when generating the initial random noise on CPUs. Through a large-scale empirical analysis conducted on images shared via the popular platform CivitAI, we demonstrate that approximately 95% of these images' seed values can be effectively brute-forced in 140 minutes per seed using our seed-recovery tool, SeedSnitch. Leveraging the recovered seed, we propose PromptPirate, a genetic algorithm-based optimization method explicitly designed for prompt stealing. PromptPirate surpasses state-of-the-art methods, i.e., PromptStealer, P2HP, and CLIP-Interrogator, achieving an 8-11% improvement in LPIPS similarity. Furthermore, we introduce straightforward and effective countermeasures that render seed stealing, and thus optimization-based prompt stealing, ineffective. We have disclosed our findings responsibly and initiated coordinated mitigation efforts with the developers to address this critical vulnerability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
